FE-only** (`apps/web/src/lib/auth.tsx` + its test): no migration (head stays `0063`), no new permission key (catalog 102), no contract change, **no Keycloak-realm change** — `redirect_uri` stays `${origin}/`; the path rides the OIDC `state`, which the oidc-client-ts *stateStore* already round-trips across the full-page redirect (only the *userStore* is in-memory; that's why PKCE works today). The SPA holds tokens in memory, so a logged-out open of any deep route auto-`login()`d through Keycloak — but `login()` was a bare `signinRedirect()` and the callback restored via `replaceState(window.location.pathname)` (= `/` at callback time) → the email `prefs_link` (`/settings/notifications`) + every notification-email deep-link landed on **Home**. Fix: `login()` stashes `window.location.pathname + window.location.search` in the OIDC **`state.returnTo`**; the `signinRedirectCallback` handler restores it via react-router `navigate(safeReturnTo(user.state.returnTo), {replace:true})` (also strips `?code&state`); **`safeReturnTo`** is an open-redirect guard accepting ONLY a same-origin absolute path (`//host`, absolute URLs, `/\`, non-strings → `/`; navigation is via react-router, never `window.location` → defense-in-depth). The `catch` branch is unchanged (a failed callback still strips the query + falls through logged-out — never wedges sign-in); `setReady(true)` is always reached.

⚠ **Traps (load-bearing):** (1) **the bootstrap `useEffect` must stay once-on-mount** — the initial `[navigate]` dep re-ran it on EVERY in-app navigation (react-router's `useNavigate` returns a function whose identity changes per pathname → redundant `getUser()` + AuthContext value churn across every consumer); fix = a `navRef` latest-ref (`const navRef = useRef(navigate); navRef.current = navigate;`, call `navRef.current(...)` in the effect) + `[]` deps. **`exhaustive-deps` is NOT enforced in this repo** (no `eslint-plugin-react-hooks` in `eslint.config.js`), so `[]` is lint-clean — do NOT add `[navigate]`. The whole-branch (opus) review caught this; a regression test confirms it (navigate once → `getUser` stays at 1 call; the test FAILS 2-vs-1 against the old `[navigate]`). (2) **the login path is load-bearing — the unit tests (MemoryRouter) CANNOT exercise the real `BrowserRouter` + Keycloak round-trip; the LIVE re-login is the gate** (verified live: `/settings/notifications` + `/risks` deep-loads land on-route via a silent SSO round-trip; `/` lands Home — no regression to normal login). (3) react-router `navigate` itself collapses an off-origin value to a same-origin path (`//evil.com`→`/evil.com`), so `safeReturnTo` is belt-and-suspenders, not the sole boundary.

**Build + review:** subagent-driven (Task 1 the `safeReturnTo` pure guard · Task 2 the `login()`/callback wiring + the vi.mock tests · Task 3 gate+review), fresh implementer + spec/quality review per task; a fix wave for the opus Important (the `navRef`) + 2 safety tests. `diff-critic` **CLEAN**, whole-branch (opus) **Ready-to-merge** (its lone Important fixed), the fix re-review **Approved**; full `/check-web` green; the live re-login passed (owner Keycloak login). **No named residuals** — the slice is self-contained. **Tests:** web **1238→1246 (+8)** (`safeReturnTo` ×3, login-capture, callback-restore, guard-applied, catch-path fall-through, no-refetch-on-navigation regression); api unchanged (**956**). (S-deeplink-login, FE-only, no migration [head `0063`], web 1238→1246, api 956, PR #259 squash `61dc442`.)

### S-notify-fe — the SPA notification bell + center (slice 2; FE-only; PR #257 squash `6893c3e`)

**What shipped — the in-app awareness UI for the slice-1 spine, FE-only:** no migration (head stays `0063`), no new permission key (catalog stays **102** — the reads are authenticated-self, so the SPA adds ZERO gating beyond signed-in), no contract change (the endpoints were already documented in S-notify-1's `openapi.yaml`). A new `apps/web/src/features/notifications/` dir consuming the existing self-scoped endpoints:
- `deepLink.ts` — a pure `toRoutePath` relativising the **absolute** `deep_link` (`app_base_url`+fragment) → `pathname+search`, with a `/tasks` parse-failure fallback; navigated **in-app** via react-router (no open-redirect surface).
- `hooks.ts` — `useNotificationCount` (the badge — the ONLY polled query, `refetchInterval 60_000`, `unread_only=true&limit=100`, mirrors `useAckCount`'s **never-confident-zero** [count read only behind `isError`]); `useNotifications("recent"|"all", enabled)` (popover gated `enabled: opened`); `useNotificationPreferences`.
- `mutations.ts` — `useMarkRead`/`useMarkAllRead` invalidate the `["notifications"]` prefix (badge + lists refresh together); `useSetEmailEnabled` invalidates `["notification-preferences"]`; **no optimistic updates**.
- `NotificationItem` — one row: unread = a **dot glyph + a `VisuallyHidden "Unread"` + bold title** (never colour alone, DP-5); the row is a `Link` with **NO explicit aria-label** (so the nested "Unread" isn't swallowed by the ARIA name computation); the "Mark read" `ActionIcon` is a **SIBLING** of the Link (no nested-interactive); body rendered as a **text node** (XSS-safe, no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`).
- `NotificationBell` — the merged TopBar bell: an `Indicator`-wrapped `ActionIcon` (badge three-state — numeric / gray indeterminate dot on a failed count / nothing on a true zero; "99+" cap) opening a `Popover` (recent 15 + Mark-all-read / Notification settings / See all).
- `NotificationsPage` (`/notifications`) — full list (50), Mark-all-read, calm `lib/states`, a "showing the 50 most recent" honesty footnote.
- `NotificationSettingsPage` (`/settings/notifications`) — the minimal master email toggle on `GET/PUT /me/notification-preferences`, **un-breaking the email `prefs_link`** slice 1 left dangling.
- Shell wiring: `TopBar` swaps the standalone ack-count `Indicator` for `<NotificationBell/>` (the **Tasks** icon stays the WORK entry; `useAckCount` is **KEPT** — Home's `DoCard` still imports it) + an Account-menu "Notification settings" item; `App.tsx` +2 routes under the AppShell layout.

**Owner decisions (AskUserQuestion ×4):** **merge into one bell** (the ack-count badge is retired — DOC_ACK assignments now flow as notifications, so the one bell's unread badge encompasses new-ack awareness; the durable open-ack work-count stays at `/tasks`) · **popover + full page** · **ship the minimal master-email-toggle now** · **60s poll + refetch on open**.

⚠ **Traps carried (load-bearing):** (1) **the strict-`tsc` trap** — `HttpResponse.json(body)` where `body: unknown` fails the full `/check-web` `tsc` gate (`JsonBodyType`) but a **per-file `vitest` run is GREEN** (the classic blind spot); cast `body as Record<string, unknown>`. (2) A Mantine `Menu`/`Popover` opened in a test needs `userEvent.setup()` + an **async** `findByRole("menuitem", …)` (the ingestion-test idiom) — do **NOT** add `transitionProps={{ duration: 0 }}` to the **production** component to force a test green (caught + reverted in review). (3) The count query uses `limit=100`, so the badge shows "99+" only at ≥100 unread (cosmetic; spec §5.1 said 99). (4) A new routed page needs a jest-axe smoke (24/28 page-tests carry one — added in the review fix wave).

**Build + review:** subagent-driven (7 impl tasks + a test-coverage fix wave), fresh implementer + spec/quality review per task; **whole-branch (opus) = Ready-to-merge**, `diff-critic` + `web-test-trap-reviewer` **CLEAN** (0 Critical/Important). **Live-smoke (Chrome MCP, owner login, org ORG_EXAMPLE):** the core loop verified end-to-end against the running stack — badge (incl. a **REAL** `task.assigned` notification, the IPR-GEN-001 approval, rendered alongside seeded rows), popover unread/read distinction, deep-link click → mark-read + **navigate to a distinct route** (`/risks`) + badge decrement (DB-persisted), mark-all-read → silent-zero (DB unread=0), the settings toggle → "Saved." (DB `email_enabled=false`), ErrorState-with-retry on token expiry, silent SSO re-auth. The `/notifications` page + the Account-menu item were **covered-by-tests-only** (the documented Mantine-portal Chrome-MCP flakiness — menus/popovers focus but don't reliably open under automation); the `/settings/notifications` route itself WAS reached live via the popover footer link.

**Codex (1 P2, owner-DEFERRED — not faked):** the SPA **loses the requested path through the Keycloak login round-trip** (`redirect_uri=${origin}/`, bare `signinRedirect`, callback `replaceState`→`/`) → a logged-out open of `/settings/notifications` or ANY notification-email deep-link lands on **Home** (so fork-3's `prefs_link` goal + all email deep-links are only half-realized). Confirmed valid end-to-end; deferred to a focused **S-deeplink-login** follow-up (a cross-cutting shared-`AuthProvider` change — benefits the `prefs_link` + every notification deep-link + bookmarks, out of this FE-only slice's scope). Spec drafted: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-22-s-deeplink-login-design.md` (stash `pathname+search` in the OIDC `state`, `navigate(returnTo, {replace:true})` after `signinRedirectCallback`, same-origin `startsWith("/")` open-redirect guard + an auth test). Replied + resolved on the PR thread.

**Named residuals (not faked):** **S-deeplink-login** (the Codex P2, above) · **objective/MR subtype deep-link routing** in `subjects.py` (a small BE follow-up; `/documents/{id}` is correct + reachable today) · the **digest matrix + quiet hours + DOC_ACK email** (slice 3) · **SSE** replacing the 60s poll (slice 5-ish) · the **retired header ack-work-count** (an intentional consequence of the merge). **Tests:** web **1210→1238 (+28)** (deepLink 4 · hooks 8 · NotificationItem 5 · NotificationBell 6 · NotificationsPage 3 · NotificationSettingsPage 3, minus the rewritten TopBar suite's net); api unchanged (**956**). (S-notify-fe, FE-only, no migration [head `0063`], web 1210→1238, api 956, PR #257 squash `6893c3e`.)

### S-notify-1 — the BE notification spine + email delivery (R53; migration 0063; BE-only; PR #255 squash `766dc55`)

**What shipped — a transactional-outbox notification spine, BE-only (no SPA).** `migration 0063` (head
`0062→0063`) creates **4 operational/mutable tables** (`notification` in-app durable · `notification_email`
the email delivery ledger · `notification_template` versioned+seeded · `notification_preference` the
per-user toggle), a fresh `notification_email_status` enum, the per-org `system_config.notifications_email_enabled`
opt-in flag, and **2 seeded `en` templates** (`task.assigned` + `system.email_delivery_failed`). A new
`services/notifications/` package: `render` (a **logic-free, HTML-escaped, whitelisted** renderer — `{{var}}`
+ `{{var|date}}`, **no eval/Jinja**, single `re.sub` pass so an injected value can't re-expand) · `subjects`
(polymorphic resolver + deep links, 7 subject types + a `/tasks` fallback) · `recipients` · `dispatch` (the
**SAVEPOINT enqueue**) · `mail` (a `MailSender` protocol + `aiosmtplib` `SmtpMailSender` + a test fake — the
`LoggingRenderSink`/`GotenbergRenderSink` split: the api never sends) · `drain` (the worker core) · `admins`.
The `outbox_drain` **Beat task** (120s). Self-scoped `GET /notifications` + mark-read + `read-all` +
`GET/PUT /me/notification-preferences`; the admin opt-in flag rides the existing `config.update`-gated
`/admin/config` (audited `CONFIG_UPDATED`). OpenAPI updated. **NO new permission key** (R38; catalog stays
102 — reads are authenticated-self, the `GET /tasks` posture). **NO WORM touch** (the 4 tables are
operational like `task` — no hash chain, no `signature_event`, no blob bytes).

**The load-bearing contract (spec §4, the crux).** Enqueue runs **inside the caller's task-creation txn under
a SAVEPOINT** (`begin_nested()`): atomic-on-success, and ANY failure (missing template, render bug) rolls
back ONLY the savepoint + logs → the **parent task txn is untouched, so a notification bug can never block a
workflow transition**. The in-app row inserts via `pg_insert(...).on_conflict_do_nothing(index_elements,
index_where="task_id IS NOT NULL").returning(id)` (the dedup partial index — the ON CONFLICT needs
`index_where` to match it); the email row inserts **only if the notification returned a new id** (a dedup hit
→ no orphan). **Send is the async drain**: it **claims ONE row at a time** (`SELECT … LIMIT 1 FOR UPDATE SKIP
LOCKED` per iteration — the lock is held until *its own* lease commits, so no sibling-lock-release
double-send), re-checks eligibility at send (org flag + recipient active + opt-out + `smtp_host` →
`SUPPRESSED` for a mid-window change), then a **count-before-send lease** (`attempts++` + a `next_attempt_at`
backoff + commit BEFORE the SMTP call, so a post-send crash can't loop unboundedly); on exhaustion → `FAILED`
(durable BEFORE the best-effort emit) + a **`system.email_delivery_failed`** in-app notification to admins
(operational-only body — NO subject title/identifier, since admins hold no `document.read`; R32). **Email is
opt-in per org (default OFF)** and carries **summary + deep link only — never controlled content** (the
deep_link resolves back into the vault). **DB-backed versioned templates**; the producing template
id+version is snapshotted per message.

**`task.assigned` wired at all 6 task-creation sites.** The engine `_materialize_stage` enqueues per-task
after its flush but **skips `DOC_ACK`/`PERIODIC_REVIEW`** — those two sweeps patch `due_at` AFTER
materialization, so they re-notify post-patch (else the email shows `Due: (none)`); the 3 direct-add sites
(`instantiate_approval`, MR cadence, MR-action spawn) enqueue inline. **DOC_ACK email is deferred to slice 3**
(in-app only now — a `subject_type` gate at the email-enqueue; avoids an onboarding email flood with no
digest yet). The welded single-stage `test_approval` stayed green (12/12). Recipients = assignee else each
pool member, **org-scoped + active-only**.

**R53 (binding) recorded** + **doc 10 W2/§156 reconciled** — the in-txn SAVEPOINT outbox **supersedes** the
older "enqueue side-effects after commit" wording (it closes the commit→enqueue crash gap; still
non-blocking). Reads add **no new permission key**.

⚠ **Traps a future session must carry.** (1) **`0010`'s `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES … GRANT … DELETE` grants the
app role ALL DML on EVERY new table** → an explicit `GRANT` block is a no-op; to enforce the intended posture
you must **`REVOKE`** (ledgers `REVOKE DELETE`; `notification_template` `REVOKE INSERT,UPDATE,DELETE` →
SELECT-only). The migration-reviewer "confirmed SELECT-only grants" in the first pass — but that was only the
explicit grants; the default-privileges override was caught by **Codex**. (2) The empty-`smtp_host` →
`SUPPRESSED` short-circuit (added so an org that enables email without configuring SMTP doesn't storm FAILED
rows) **broke 3 `test_notification_e2e` drain tests in CI** (CI has no `SMTP_HOST`) — while a **local `.env`
with `SMTP_HOST=mailpit` MASKED it** (the e2e tests called `drain_once(…, get_settings(), …)`; the sibling
`test_notification_drain` had been updated to a `_configured_settings()` helper, the e2e file was missed).
**Lesson: a settings-dependent test must pin the setting, not read the ambient env; re-run the WHOLE touched
suite after a fix, and reproduce CI by unsetting the env (`env -u SMTP_HOST`).** (3) The drain's original
**batch `LIMIT N` claim + per-row commit released sibling `FOR UPDATE` locks** → an overlapping drain
(runtime > 120s beat) could double-send a sibling — flagged by THREE reviewers (Task-8/opus/Codex) before
being fixed (claim-one-row). (4) Email **header CR/LF** in a rendered subject raises `ValueError` → the drain
mis-handles it as transient → false `FAILED` → fold CR/LF (`_header_safe`).

**Process.** Spec-first under `docs/superpowers/specs/` + a **5-lens pre-code refute panel** that killed 6
design flaws before any migration (unbounded-resend, an admin-metadata leak, the polymorphic-hook scope, the
post-flush `due_at`, the DOC_ACK flood, the canon-timing reconciliation). **13-task subagent-driven build**
(`docs/superpowers/plans/`), each spec+quality reviewed; final reviews migration-reviewer + diff-critic +
opus whole-branch all CLEAN (the opus review's lone "Important" — a `cadence.py` missing flush —
**empirically refuted** by the diff-critic: SQLAlchemy restores a parent-pending object after a savepoint
rollback). **Live-smoke** on the rebuilt image: the real `aiosmtplib`→Mailpit delivery verified (`SENT`,
the rendered email with `|date` + deep link, no controlled content). **2 Codex rounds, 9 findings → 7 fixed
(both P1s round 1: claim-one-row drain + the REVOKE; round 2: recipients org-scope + the CR/LF fold; +
inactive filter + at-send opt-out re-check), 2 deferred** (the `prefs_link` `/settings/notifications` route →
slice 3; objective/MR subtype deep-link routing → a UX refinement, the generic `/documents/{id}` is reachable
by the approval recipient who holds `document.*`).

**Named residuals (not faked):** slices 2–5 (above); the deferred objective/MR **subtype deep-link routing**;
the **`prefs_link`** route (slice 3); the informational **INVITED-not-in-`_INACTIVE`** note (a faithful mirror
of `auth/dependencies.py` — an INVITED account could be emailed; reconciled to ACTIVE on first login).

(S-notify-1, BE-only, migration **0063** [head 0062→0063], api unit **952→956** [+4 mail header-fold tests; +
notification unit across the slice] + many notification integration/e2e, web unchanged, PR #255 squash `766dc55`.)

## REGISTER STEWARD ROLE — the reserved role making the three register-steward consoles self-service (R52; migration 0062)

> Closes the **F-1 deferral** named across R49/R50/R51: the in-app register-steward consoles (Risk 6.1 / Context 4.1 / Interested Parties 4.2) shipped FE-only (S-risk-5 · S-context-fe · S-interested-parties-fe) gating on the SERVER-computed `register_capabilities`, but their lifecycle acts (start-revision / publish / **release**) were exercisable **only via a SYSTEM override** in v1. This seeds a dedicated reserved role so stewardship is self-service end-to-end — a cross-cutting authz capstone serving all three register families.

**S-register-steward = a reserved `Register Steward` role (R52; migration 0062 [head 0061→0062]; BE-only).** The role holds the full stewardship set at **SYSTEM** scope — `register.read · register.manage · document.release · document.read · document.read_draft` — and deliberately **EXCLUDES `document.approve`** (SoD: the approver stays a separate Approver/QMS-Owner; register publish still routes its approval to that pool, and release stays releaser ≠ approver). **It is the FIRST seeded role to hold `document.release`** → v1 release authority becomes **role-grantable**, not SYSTEM-override-only (R52). **NO new permission key** (catalog stays 102; R38-additive covers a new role + grants on existing keys), **NO contract change, NO FE change** — the consoles already gate their Publish/Start-revision/Release affordances on the server-computed `register_capabilities`, so the role lights up the affordances automatically + auto-appears in `GET /roles` for an admin to assign. ⚠ **THE reframe a future session must carry: `register.manage` was ALREADY seeded to QMS Owner @ SYSTEM (`0004` line 185) — so start-revision/publish already worked for a QMS-Owner user; the genuine gap was `document.release`, granted to NO seeded role (`seed_personas.py` says it outright) → release of EVERY document (normal docs, POL/OBJ/MR, the register heads) is SYSTEM-override-only by deliberate v1 posture. The slice's substance is therefore role-grantable RELEASE authority, not register.manage.** ⚠ **Migration trap: the seed MUST use the resilient name-agnostic org lookup (`SELECT id FROM organization` → the sole org, D1) — NOT `0004`'s bare `WHERE short_code='DEFAULT'` `scalar_one()`, which aborts on a renamed OPERATIONAL install (this install: `ORG_EXAMPLE`; `services/common/org.py` + the 0053/0054/0057 resilient-lookup precedent).** Data-only (no DDL / no ORM change → `alembic check` clean); idempotent `on_conflict_do_nothing` on `(org_id, name)` / `(org_id, role_id, permission_id)` (the live UNIQUE constraints); downgrade deletes the steward's `role_assignment` → `role_grant` → `role` (both FKs to `role.id` are `ondelete=RESTRICT`), scoped by name — the migration-reviewer **empirically round-tripped this on a live PG16 incl. the CI-blind populated-DB downgrade [attached a real `role_assignment`, downgrade EXIT 0, `permission` held 102→102, the 8 starter roles intact] + the renamed-`ORG_EXAMPLE`-org path.** **4 owner decisions (AskUserQuestion ×4): next slice = this; release authority = a NEW dedicated role (over extending QMS Owner — which conflates approve+release and collides with the approval stage — or a narrow new `register.release` key — R38 catalog-open + a backend gate change across the 3 release endpoints); the FULL stewardship key set; record as binding R52 + a spec.** **Tests (all integration, testcontainer PG16): `test_authz` seed assertion (the exact 10-role set [+ Register Steward] + the steward grant map [5 keys @ SYSTEM via exact-equality, no `document.approve`]; catalog stays 102); a role-ONLY Risk lifecycle end-to-end in `test_risk_lifecycle` (`s5.grant_role` only, NO `_grant` override → publish → a SEPARATE Approver approves → a third-party steward releases → Effective; the steward is 403 self-approving [role excludes `document.approve`]; SoD-2 gives the author-steward `can_release:false` while the third-party steward releaser gets `true`); a new `test_register_steward_role.py` (the role reaches CTX/IP `can_manage`; + the LEADERSHIP NON-REGRESSION — a role-only steward releasing an Approved OBJ with the flag ON is still `409 leadership_authorization_required`, asserted by the EXACT code [a generic non-200 would false-pass a 403] with the flag reset in `finally`, proving `document.release @ SYSTEM` does NOT bypass the Top-Management gate).** ⚠ **Authz facts proven end-to-end (diff-critic + the whole-branch review traced these): the approval candidate pool is resolved by ROLE NAME (the workflow stage `assignees.roles`), so excluding `document.approve` genuinely keeps the steward out of the pool; SoD-2 is an app-level overlay independent of the grant (the author-steward computes `can_release:false`); the leadership preflight runs inside `_cutover` AFTER the `enforce(document.release)`, so a role-only releaser passes authz but is blocked by the gate.** **Named-not-faked deferrals: a narrow `register.release` key (least-privilege — the owner chose the role-grant lane); NO 9th persona / `seed-personas` steward (the role is left for an admin to assign — the live-smoke assigns it to a fresh user).** Built via **subagent-driven development** (5 tasks, each implement → spec+quality review → fix-loop; 1 Important docs finding [the new `docs/14 §3.1` roster omitted Top Management] fixed-forward to all 10 roles). diff-critic + migration-reviewer + a whole-branch senior review **ALL CLEAN** (0 Critical/Important); full `/check-api` green (925 unit). (S-register-steward, BE-only, migration **0062** [head 0061→0062], api unit unchanged [925], +1 integration assertion [`test_authz`] / +1 integration test [`test_risk_lifecycle`] / +2 integration tests [new `test_register_steward_role.py`], web unchanged [~1210], PR #253 squash `284d6e1`.)

## INTERESTED PARTIES REGISTER — clause 4.2 Needs & expectations of interested parties (the THIRD register family; R51; migration 0061)

> The third "register" family and the second half of clause 4 "Context of the organization" — reusing R49/R50's register-as-Document pattern **verbatim** (a near-byte-identical clone of the Context register, the simpler categorical sibling of Risk). SPEC-LIGHTER than S-risk-spec (the architecture is settled) — owner decisions surfaced via AskUserQuestion before code. **Org-level, NOT process-scoped** (clause 4.2 interested parties are strategic/org-wide; the Context divergence from risk). **Core + lifecycle (S-interested-parties-1) + the read consumers (S-interested-parties-2 — the `GET /summary` governing read + the MR 9.3.2(b) consumer sourcing BOTH the 4.1 + 4.2 halves, finally closing the `CONTEXT_CHANGES` sourceless gap S-context-2 carried here) are now SHIPPED; **S-interested-parties-fe ships the SPA → R51 is COMPLETE end-to-end (core · lifecycle · the governing summary + MR consumer · the SPA + steward console).**

[a completeness prompt, the SWOT fixed-frame analogue]); `InterestedPartyScorecardBand` (the CLIENT-side working-view rollup — active/total + the high/medium/low/unspecified influence distribution + never-reviewed amber); `InterestedPartyDrawer` (NO CAPA seam — clause 4.2 has no treatment axis; shows party_type/influence/status badges + `party_name` + `needs_expectations` + last-reviewed + Edit); `New`/`EditPartyModal` (a 7-way `party_type` Select [NOT a SegmentedControl] + `party_name` TextInput + `needs_expectations` Textarea + a clearable ordered `influence` Select + native `type=date`; partial PATCH — omitted ≠ null, explicit null clears influence/last_reviewed_at); `RegisterLifecyclePanel` + `PublishRegisterModal` (the steward console — start-revision/publish/release gated STATE × the SERVER `can_manage`/`can_release` caps; approve/decide rides the existing `/tasks` DOCUMENT arm, ZERO new FE). ## CONTEXT REGISTER — clause 4.1 Understanding the organization & its context (the SECOND register family; R50; migration 0060)

> The second "register" family, reusing R49's register-as-Document pattern verbatim (the first reuse the pattern was explicitly set for). SPEC-LIGHTER than S-risk-spec (the architecture is settled) — 4 owner decisions surfaced via AskUserQuestion before code. **Org-level, NOT process-scoped** (clause 4.1 context is strategic/org-wide; the deliberate divergence from risk, matching the doc-14 §6 contract that omits `process_id` on `context_issue`). **Now COMPLETE end-to-end** — core+lifecycle (S-context-1) · the governing-summary read consumer (S-context-2) · the SPA + steward console (S-context-fe). The clause-4.2 Interested Parties register (R51) core+lifecycle now ships as **S-interested-parties-1** (its own family section above); the remaining context-adjacent residual is **S-interested-parties-2** (the MR 9.3.2(b) input wired sourcing both the 4.1 + 4.2 governing summaries — `CONTEXT_CHANGES` stays a sourceless gap until then).

The 3 enums + the additive `CONTEXT_ISSUE_UPDATED` event type + the `CTX` document_type seed (`is_singleton`, all orgs). `status` is NOT NULL with **no server_default** (the service supplies `active` on insert — greenfield, sidesteps the server_default alembic-check trap). ## RISK & OPPORTUNITY REGISTER — clause 6.1 Risks & Opportunities (the FIRST register family; R18/R49; migration 0058–0059)

> The largest remaining core-ISO gap + the **FIRST "register" family** (sets the register-as-Document pattern Context 4.1 / Interested Parties 4.2 reuse). SPEC-FIRST (a register-as-Document architectural fork + a new-vs-existing-keys call = owner's). Builds on the now-complete process-scope authz arc (below): risk rows carry `process_id`, **own-id-only per R48**.

- **S-risk-5** (the in-app register-steward lifecycle console — closes the F-1 deferral S-risk-4b named; **with it the clause-6.1 family is not just complete end-to-end but FULLY SELF-SERVICE in-app** — the steward no longer needs the API / a SYSTEM override to run the register's controlled-document cycle) — **a "Register lifecycle" card on `RisksRegisterPage` that drives the THREE steward-only acts (start-revision → publish → release); the approve/decide step keeps riding the existing `/tasks` DOCUMENT arm (an RSK version is a `subject_type=DOCUMENT` task → `ReviewApprovePage`'s generic redline + `DecisionCard`) with ZERO new FE.** **What shipped (FE-only, NO migration [head stays `0059`], NO new permission key [rides `register.manage` / `document.release`, catalog 102], NO contract change — the 3 lifecycle endpoints already shipped in S-risk-1b + were documented in `openapi.yaml`):** (1) **`RegisterLifecyclePanel`** — a bordered card rendered only for a steward (`canManage || canRelease`, quiet absence for everyone else), gating each button on STATE × permission (the RSK head has NO server `capabilities` block, unlike OBJ): **Publish** on Draft/UnderRevision, **Start revision** on Effective, **Release** on Approved, an "in review — an approver decides in Tasks" note (+ a `/tasks` link) on InReview. Release routes through the shared `ConfirmDestructive` (stays-open-on-409 surfaces the SoD-2 self-release reason); start-revision fires directly (reversible posture, the OBJ Lifecycle-Card idiom). (2) **`PublishRegisterModal`** — an optional change-reason `Textarea` (recorded on the frozen version + shown to the approver; the server defaults a system reason when blank), conditionally mounted so close resets the draft, `onClose` guarded while pending. (3) **`mutations.ts`** — `useStartRiskRegisterRevision` / `usePublishRiskRegister` / `useReleaseRiskRegister` + a shared invalidate closure (`risk-register` / `risks` / `risks-summary`). (4) **`RisksRegisterPage`** renders the panel; the read-only banner stays informational for non-stewards (the console, not the shared banner, carries the CTA — preserving the S-risk-4b "don't instruct what a viewer can't do" rule). **Gating asymmetry (load-bearing):** start-revision/publish probe **`register.manage` @ SYSTEM** (the backend `_register_manage_system` FORCES SYSTEM — a bound Process-Owner can't steward the org head); release probes **`document.release` at the head's EFFECTIVE ARTIFACT scope** (`register_doc_id`), NOT SYSTEM. **⚠ Codex was the authz edge-finder again (2 rounds, 3 P2 fixed + 1 declined):** round-1 — (a) the SYSTEM-only release probe MISSED an artifact-scoped `document.release` override the backend's `_register_release_scope` (which builds a doc-resource scope) WOULD authorize → **probe at the head's effective ARTIFACT scope (it folds matching SYSTEM grants + scoped DENYs — the S-risk-4b doctrine)**; - **S-risk-1b** (the controlled-document publish/freeze/release lifecycle — closes the named S-risk-1 deferral) — **the RSK register head becomes a true controlled Effective document: risk rows are version CONTENT, edited through FSM revisions, FROZEN (rows + per-method scoring criteria) into immutable versions at publish; the live band grades against the GOVERNING version's frozen criteria, never live code (R49 L2; the objectives derive-and-freeze precedent verbatim).** **What shipped (NO migration [head stays `0058`], NO new permission key [catalog 102]; contract = 4 new `/risks/register/*` endpoints + `RiskRegisterStatus`/`RiskRegisterPublish` schemas):** (1) **Pure `domain/risk/register_content.py`** — `build_register({rows,criteria})` / `criteria_for_methods` / `resolve_criteria(governing, scoring_method)` / `register_needs_freeze` (mirrors `domain/objectives/commitment.py`; rows sorted by id for a reproducible WORM body; criteria a per-method map keyed by `scoring_method.value`). (2) **The content-aware WORM freeze (`services/vault/service.py`)** — `_snapshot(risk_register=…)` (additive; ∉ `SNAPSHOT_FIELDS` → invisible to the DCR metadata diff) + `checkin_risk_register` (the `checkin_objective_commitment` verbatim: `rfc8785.dumps` JCS → staging-PUT `application/json` → `finalize_worm` → `metadata_snapshot.risk_register`; **FLUSH not commit** [publish owns the txn], `application/json` non-renderable R26, `rendition_blob_sha256` left NULL [never the mirror's controlled-copy pointer], blob-iff-bytes held). (3) **`services/risk/lifecycle.py`** — `start_register_revision` (T7 wrapper over the vault `start_revision`) + `publish_register` (the `submit_objective_for_review` shape: head FOR-UPDATE+populate_existing → `register_needs_freeze` → `checkin_risk_register` → drop the start-revision WorkingDraft [O-4] → `submit_review` → `instantiate_approval` → audit → commit → release the edit lock); `queries.governing_register` resolves the head's Effective version's frozen `risk_register` (PRIOR Effective while UnderRevision — R43). (4) **`api/risk.py`** — `POST /risks/register/{start-revision,publish,release}` + `GET /risks/register` status; the `_risk` serializer grades the band via `resolve_criteria(governing, …)`; the static `/risks/register*` routes mount BEFORE `/risks/{risk_id}` (S-pack-2). **Two owner decisions (AskUserQuestion):** (D-2b) **`register.manage` @ SYSTEM gates start-revision/publish** — the QMS Owner (holds it @ SYSTEM) stewards the register, a bound Process-Owner's PROCESS grant can't publish the org head; release on **`document.release` @ SYSTEM + SoD-2** (the OBJ release posture; SYSTEM override in v1). The OBJ surface-key precedent over the spec §2's loose "document.* SYSTEM override". (D-3b) **fold the managed-doc reservation in.** **Investigated, no config needed:** `instantiate_approval` is keyed by `WorkflowSubjectType.DOCUMENT` (the `document_approval` def) — **doc-type-agnostic** → RSK rides the SAME lightweight Approver/QMS-Owner stage OBJ/MR ride; an empty pool → `NEEDS_ATTENTION`. **The D-3b reservation, completed under Codex pressure — the RSK head is now reserved from EVERY content/lifecycle mutation:** generic metadata-PATCH / distribution / document-link (both branches + the link **target** + delete other-end), clause-mapping map/unmap (RSK-only), obsoletion at the **`lifecycle.obsolete` CHOKEPOINT** (covers the generic `/obsolete` route AND a RETIRE DCR that calls `obsolete()` directly), and **DCR targeting** at `_resolve_target` (RSK-only — closes a REVISE-DCR adopting the publish-Approved version + releasing it past the `/risks/register/release` SoD-2). The generic `/documents/{id}/release` is intentionally left open (authz-equivalent: same `document.release`+SoD-2+cutover, not a bypass). **⚠ THE structural lesson — the RSK head is a per-org SINGLETON shared across the one-org integration DB**, so a lifecycle test that drives it to Effective would 409 every OTHER test's risk-create (and break `test_risk_process_scope`); there is NO runtime multi-org bootstrap → the head-advancing test NORMALIZES the shared head to editable at the start + a **`restore_register_head` teardown fixture** returns it to UnderRevision even if the body fails mid-lifecycle (a `MissingGreenlet`-500 after `release()` had already committed the head Effective was the cascade CI shard-4 caught). **⚠ Codex was THE edge-finder again over 3 rounds a CLEAN diff-critic ×2 + green CI MISSED — 6 genuine fixes; the re-posts are STALE duplicates (the fixes present + CI-proven by the held-lock/reservation tests) → trim-don't-chase + ask the owner, don't loop:** round-1 (1 P1 + 3 P2): **the P1 version-integrity TOCTOU** — a concurrent `POST/PATCH /risks` row write could land AFTER publish freezes the version (row writers didn't hold publish's head lock), leaving live rows out of the SIGNED version → `add_risk_row`/`update_risk_row` now lock the head **FOR UPDATE** (consistent row→head order; publish locks only the head → deadlock-free; the S-records-W discipline), proven by a 2-session held-lock-blocks test; + the link-target / clause-mapping reservations. round-2 (1 new): the RETIRE-DCR obsolete bypass → the chokepoint. round-3 (1 new): **the empty-register publish race** — the first `POST /risks` commits the 0-row head in `resolve_or_create_head` BEFORE taking the head FOR UPDATE to insert, so a publish that locks the head in that window freezes an EMPTY version + 409s (loses) the first risk → `publish_register` rejects a zero-row register under the lock (closes the race + a sensible business rule). Plus the **release endpoint `MissingGreenlet`** (read status off `release()`'s `_cutover`-refreshed doc, NOT `expire_all`+re-read — the sync `_register_status` lazy-loaded otherwise). **⚠ Traps a future session MUST carry:** (1) the per-org-singleton head makes lifecycle tests pollute siblings → normalize-at-start + restore-on-teardown; (2) row writes must lock the head FOR UPDATE to serialize against the publish freeze (the version must contain EXACTLY the signed rows); (3) reserve the managed head at the SERVICE chokepoint when a path can reach it both via an endpoint AND a DCR (`lifecycle.obsolete`); the head-steward 403 [PROCESS owner]; the reservation 422s [metadata/distribution/links/clause-mapping/obsolete/DCR-target + OBJ]; the held-lock race); **web unchanged.** ruff/format/mypy-strict clean (modulo the Windows `O_NOFOLLOW` baseline); redocly clean; CI 11/11 green (all 4 shards). **diff-critic CLEAN ×2 (original + final full-diff); Codex 3 rounds (6 fixed, all threads addressed/resolved).** **Merge gate:** green CI + Codex threads resolved + owner's explicit go. - **S-risk-1** (backend register core — model + migration + rules + service + `/risks` routes + openapi + tests) — **a working risk register: authorized users author/score/treat/read process-scoped risk rows with every load-bearing authz/re-derive invariant the spec's 5-lens panel surfaced.** **What shipped (migration `0058`, NO new permission key — rides the already-seeded `register.read`/`register.manage` [`0004:97-98`]; catalog stays 102; contract = the `/risks` endpoints + 6 schemas):** (1) **`risk_opportunity`** = a **1:many satellite** of a `kind=DOCUMENT` `RSK` head (own `id` PK + `register_doc_id` FK + `row_version`; NOT a shared-PK subtype like `quality_objective` — a register has many rows; the sibling `context_issue`/`interested_party` shape) carrying `type`/`description`/`process_id`/`clause_id`/`likelihood`/`severity`/`risk_rating`/`scoring_method`/`treatment`/`effectiveness`/`linked_capa_id` + the `likelihood`/`severity` BETWEEN-1-AND-5 CHECKs (bare-token names); the `risk_opportunity_type`+`scoring_method` enums + the additive `RISK_RESCORED` event type; the `RSK` document_type seed (`is_singleton`, all orgs); and **the `register.manage`→Process-Owner role grant with the `_PROCESS_SCOPE` template** (NOT `_SYSTEM_SCOPE` — the panel L1-CRITICAL: a SYSTEM template is exempt from `bound_scope` clamping → would match every process; an R38-additive grant, no new key). (2) **Pure `domain/risk/rules.py`** — `risk_rating = likelihood × severity` (1..25) + `risk_band` (total, criteria-PARAMETERIZED) + a 4-band RAG (Critical/High/Medium/Low + unscored) reusing the objectives tone/glyph vocabulary, with a **golden test** pinning each `scoring_method`'s criteria byte-shape (the L2 immutability guard). (3) **`services/risk/service.py`** — `resolve_or_create_head` (an advisory-lock-serialized get-or-create of the single non-Obsolete RSK head; the head carries **ZERO ProcessLinks** via `create_document(processes=())` — L1-MAJOR); `add_risk_row`/`update_risk_row` re-derive `risk_rating` on EVERY write (never client-supplied), gate edits to the head being Draft/UnderRevision, validate the clause against the org framework BEFORE head-create (no orphan), and **re-authorize `register.manage` over the row's CURRENT freshly-locked `process_id` after the `FOR UPDATE`** (the under-lock TOCTOU close); a re-score emits `RISK_RESCORED`. (4) **`/risks` routes** — filter-not-403 LIST (per-row `register.read`, NO `artifact_id` to avoid a shared-head leak, `source_ip` threaded), scoped GET, body-scope POST, a PATCH that gates on `process_id` **PRESENCE** (a `null` clear enforces at SYSTEM) and re-enforces the NEW target on reassign; `extra="forbid"` bodies. (5) **the RSK head reserved from generic document paths** — a shared `reject_rsk_register_mutation` (type-code based, since RSK is a satellite head not a shared-PK subtype) wired into the process-link-add path + the byte/lifecycle `reject_objective_byte_path`; the import commit rejects `type_code "RSK"`. **⚠ Scope: the controlled-document publish/freeze/approval/release/revision lifecycle (+ the governing-snapshot band resolve) is DEFERRED to S-risk-1b (named)** — the head stays Draft (a working register), the golden test the L2 protection without versioning; the spec's "partial-unique single-head index" reconciled to a **service-level advisory-lock guard** (an RSK-specific partial-unique isn't expressible without breaking POL's draft-successor flow that the Effective-only `uq_doc_info_singleton_effective` deliberately allows). **⚠ Codex was THE authz edge-finder over 3 rounds a CLEAN diff-critic + migration-reviewer MISSED — 8 real findings fixed + 3 trimmed:** round-1 (2 P1 + 2 P2): the `{process_id:null}` clear bypassed re-auth (present-but-None → gate on PRESENCE); the generic `POST /documents` could mint a process-linked/second RSK head `_find_head` adopts (reserve RSK); clause-validated-after-head-create orphaned a doc; the under-lock TOCTOU. round-2 (1 P1 + 3 P2, all "the head reachable via another generic path"): the process-link-add + byte-path + import vectors → the shared `reject_rsk_register_mutation`. round-3 (3 P2, same class): the metadata/clause-mapping/distribution/document-link endpoints → **TRIMMED as a named residual** (privileged-actor SYSTEM/ARTIFACT `document.*` edges, NOT bound-owner escalations [the escalation — a ProcessLink on the head — IS closed]; at parity with the OBJ/MR managed-doc reservation which also guards only the byte path; comprehensively reserving the managed-doc family from EVERY generic endpoint is a cross-cutting follow-up — **trim-don't-chase**). **⚠ Traps a future session MUST carry:** (1) **a SYSTEM `scope_template` is exempt from `bound_scope` clamping** (`repository._grant_from_role`) → a PROCESS-scoped ROW grant MUST use `_PROCESS_SCOPE`, never the `clauseMap.read`/`_SYSTEM_SCOPE` precedent (an org-level resource = the OPPOSITE case). (2) **the RSK head is a generic `documented_information`** every generic document endpoint can reach — reserve it (create/process-link/byte-path/import done; metadata/distribution/links trimmed). (3) **a re-derived STORED verdict needs the freeze** — the band must grade against FROZEN criteria (golden test now; governing-snapshot resolve in S-risk-1b), never live code. (4) **the ruff hook strips just-added imports → body-first** (hit repeatedly: `reject_rsk_register_mutation`, `ConfigDict`, the service authz imports). (5) `pg_advisory_xact_lock` needs explicit `CAST(:x AS integer)` (psycopg binds Python ints as bigint → no two-arg overload). **Named residuals (not faked):** the controlled-doc publish lifecycle (S-risk-1b); the metadata/distribution/document-link generic-endpoint reservation (round-3 trim, a cross-cutting managed-doc follow-up); `subject.risk_rating` workflow routing (the routing subject is the document but `risk_rating` is on the row — no resolver; v1 = stored/sortable/dashboard only); a register-steward role/UI for the org-head lifecycle. **Test delta: +24 api unit** (`test_risk_rules.py` — the 4×5=20 worked example, the criteria golden pin, band totality over 1..25) **+11 api integration** (`test_risk_process_scope.py`: list-narrows-to-owned · detail-enforce-scope · SYSTEM-read-holder · no-grant-200-empty · the Slice-W escalation [unowned-P2/null-clear/reassign all 403] · head-no-ProcessLink · single-head · re-derive + the RISK_RESCORED audit · the generic-mutation reservations [process-link/checkout 422] · unknown-field 422) **+ a `register.manage` Process-Owner grant assertion in `test_authz`** (catalog stays 102, scope_template PROCESS); **web unchanged.** ruff + format + mypy-strict clean (modulo the Windows `O_NOFOLLOW` baseline); migration round-trip up↔down↔`alembic check` CLEAN on PG16; redocly clean; CI 11/11 green (all 4 integration shards). **diff-critic + migration-reviewer CLEAN; Codex 3 rounds (8 fixed, 3 trimmed-with-rationale, all threads addressed).** **Merge gate:** green CI + Codex threads addressed (round-3 declined-with-rationale + named residual) + owner's explicit go. - **S-risk-spec** (the register-as-Document model + scoring derive-and-freeze + the permission-key decision + the slice plan, SPECCED + adversarially validated BEFORE code) — `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-19-s-risk-register-design.md` (§0–§13) + **R49** + the two self-range bumps (`R1–R48 → R1–R49`). **Three binding owner decisions (via AskUserQuestion ×2):** (D-1) **register-as-Document** — one `kind=DOCUMENT` `RSK` head holding many `risk_opportunity` satellite rows that version together (non-★ clause 6.1 is still Document-backed: **register vs progressing-activity**, NOT ★-vs-non-★; A3 binds registers to the Document lifecycle regardless of ★; improvement/10.3/R46 is the own-table activity contrast); (D-2) **5×5 matrix, stored + frozen** — `risk_rating = L×S` stored, re-derived on write, the band graded against FROZEN criteria; (D-3) **ride the seeded `register.*`** (catalog stays 102) + an R38 `register.manage`→Process-Owner grant; (D-4) **strict controlled-document** — the rows ARE version content, FSM-revision-edited. **⚠ A 5-lens adversarial refute panel found + drove the fix of TWO real flaws BEFORE any code:** (L3) the first draft's "freely-editable working satellite while Effective" had NO FSM state + inverted D2 (the controlled version lags unboundedly) — "form_template precedent exactly" was FALSE (form_template ALSO seals at Effective) → withdrawn for the strict controlled-document model (D-4, the 2nd owner question); (L2) grading the live band off current code would silently re-grade history → resolve against the governing version's FROZEN criteria + a golden test. Plus the L1 `_PROCESS_SCOPE` grant fix, the link-less head invariant, and the FOR-UPDATE CAPA-spawn lock — all carried into S-risk-1. Docs-only.
