## v1.x INCLUDE_SUBPROCESSES RECONCILE — PROCESS-grant descendant inclusion is v1.x-deferred (R48); the whole process-scope authz arc is now COMPLETE (docs-only, no migration, head stays 0057)

- **S-include-subprocesses** (reconcile `include_subprocesses` to v1 own-id-only + a binding R48 entry) — **the LAST named gap in the process-scope authz arc** (S-owner-assignment-1 [#209] → `GET /processes` row-filter [#213] → records R/W/C [#216/#217/#218] → CAPA-raise [#220] → **here**). docs/07 §5.1/§5.3/§9.1 + docs/15 §9.2 promised a `PROCESS` grant "optionally includes descendants (`include_subprocesses` flag, default true)", but the v1 PDP (`domain/authz/pdp.py` `_matches_scope`, PROCESS branch) matches PURELY on own-id intersection (`bool(scoped & resource.process_ids)`) and NEVER walks `process.parent_id` — the flag is unimplemented anywhere; owner-assignment mints an EXPLICIT `process_ids` set, and `_document_scope`/the records resolver/search/the `GET /processes`/`/map` row-filter all match on own-id/own-link sets. **SPEC-FIRST (a binding authz-model fork → owner's call, not a silent pick):** a 4-agent analysis Workflow (impl-surface map / adversarial 4-lens refute panel / doc-reconcile sweep / cost-alternatives) weighed IMPLEMENT vs RECONCILE; the refute panel returned `reopens_nonconverging_surface` (CRITICAL/unsafe) and the owner chose **RECONCILE-defer**. **What shipped (DOCS-ONLY, NO migration [head stays `0057`], NO new key, NO behavior change):** marked descendant inclusion **v1.x-deferred (v1 = own-id-only)** at the 4 forward-promise locations (07 §5.1 table PROCESS-row selector cell, §5.3 Processes inheritance bullet, §9.1 Diego persona bound-scope cell, 15 §9.2 scope-inheritance bullet) + a new **R48** decisions-register entry + bumped the register's OWN self-range `R1–R47 → R1–R48` (the intro line + the Part-3 heading). The factual `process.parent_id` self-FK schema (docs/14, the ERD `parent/subprocess` edge) is **UNCHANGED** — a process CAN nest; only its authz-grant inheritance is deferred. **⚠ WHY defer, not implement (the refute panel's CRITICAL finding):** expanding a PROCESS grant's `process_ids` to descendants at the SINGLE `gather_grants` grant-resolution chokepoint would **silently DEFEAT every per-target write re-auth guard** (`_enforce_target_process_record`, `_enforce_target_process`, the per-process `capa.create` gate) — each re-enforces against a single LITERAL target process id that the expanded grant would now intersect, re-opening exactly the non-converging records/CAPA **write-escalation** surface S-records-W deliberately trimmed to broad-only; a faithful `default true` flip would ALSO silently widen every existing bound Process-Owner grant at once (no grant carries the flag today). **⚠ The better future design (if v1.x ever implements it):** a materialized **ltree ancestry column** on `process` (mirror how FOLDER achieves ZERO-walk subtree inheritance via the `folder_path` string, R6 / `pdp.py` FOLDER branch) — its own slice (migration + backfill + reparent-rewrite + a resource-side path on EVERY `ResourceContext` builder) + its own register decision, **NOT** an on-the-fly grant-side expansion. **Verification:** the reconcile matches the proven code — the existing `test_process_owner_list_hides_unreadable_parent_id` (`tests/integration/test_process_scope.py`) already encodes own-id-only behavior (a bound child-owner does NOT reach a resource via the parent); diff-critic CLEAN (verified PDP own-id-only, all surfaces own-id/own-link, the cited test, R48 numbering/format/truthful no-migration claim, the 4-location consistency, markdown intact). **⚠ Codex P3 (the one finding): an R48 add leaves the register's own self-range labels stale at `R1–R47`** (intro + Part-3 heading) → bumped both. **⚠ A push/merge RACE dropped the P3 range-fix commit from the squash** — I pushed the fix (`7d3f401`) while the owner squash-merged the LAGGING PR head (`6e520db`); GitHub squashed the *displayed* head, so the range bump landed on the branch ref but NOT in the merged squash → **re-applied via this finish-slice follow-up** (the lesson: after a late push, VERIFY the PR head SHA before relying on a merge — a squash captures the registered head, not necessarily the branch tip). **Named residuals (NOT regressions; carried from the arc, none re-opened):** the v1.x descendant-inclusion itself (now bound as R48 + the ltree-ancestry future design); `init-upload` PROCESS-staging (S-records-W); Process-Owner CAPA/finding/document-evidence trimmed broad-only (S-records-W); the 2 round-6 correction-vs-link TOCTOUs (S-records-W); the CAPA-raise multi-role first-process-probe false-negative (S-capa-raise-process). **No test delta (docs-only; web/api counts unchanged).** PR [#222](https://github.com/CoJoA13/EasySynQ/pull/222) squash `8ba40a8` (the reconcile) + this finish-slice docs follow-up (re-applies the lost `R1–R48` range bump + the slice narrative).

## v1.x CAPA-RAISE PROCESS-SCOPE — a bound Process-Owner reaches + raises a CAPA from the board (closes the named owner-assignment CAPA-raise deferral; no migration, head stays 0057)

- **S-capa-raise-process** (wire the PROCESS-scoped CAPA raise from the board + scope the board's reads per-process) — **closes the long-deferred CAPA-Raise process-scope gap (Codex P1 on #102, named "UNBLOCKED but unwired" by S-owner-assignment-1), now exercisable because the owner-assignment binding [#209] mints a real PROCESS-scoped `capa.create` and the `GET /processes` row-filter [#213] populates the picker source.** **Scope GREW from FE-only → BE+FE mid-review** (owner-approved) when Codex showed the FE alone couldn't reach its target persona. **What shipped (BE+FE, NO migration [head stays `0057`], NO new permission key — rides `capa.create`/`capa.read`, catalog stays 102; contract = description text only):** **(FE)** (1) **`RaiseCapaModal`** gained an optional Process picker (`useProcesses()` from `objectives/hooks`, the `RaiseInitiativeModal` idiom; conditionally rendered when ≥1 process is readable) that submits `process_id` in the `CapaRaiseBody` — a process-less raise stays byte-identical to the SYSTEM/ad-hoc CAPA; (2) a `requireProcess` prop makes the picker **required** (label "Process" + placeholder "Pick the owning process"; submit gated on a pick) for a PROCESS-only creator, since a process-less raise authorizes at SYSTEM → 403; (3) the modal is now **conditionally mounted** (`{raiseOpen && <RaiseCapaModal …/>}`, the `RaiseInitiativeModal` precedent) so close unmounts + discards the draft (a picked-then-cancelled process can't bleed into the next raise); (4) **`CapaBoardPage`** Raise button shows when the caller holds `capa.create` at **SYSTEM OR their first readable process** (`usePermissions()` SYSTEM probe `||` a `usePermissions({level:"PROCESS",id:firstProcessId})` probe; `usePermissions(undefined)` dedupes to the SYSTEM query when there are no readable processes), and passes `requireProcess={!systemCanCreate}`. **(BE — the Codex P1 fix)** the board's own reads were SYSTEM-gated, so a *pure* bound Process-Owner 403'd `GET /capas` (`useCapas().forbidden` → the no-access panel) and never saw the button → applied the **S-process-scope-2 read-filter pattern** (a safe DECOUPLED read — a CAPA's `process_id` is set by an already-re-authorized `POST /capas`): (5) `GET /capas` moved from `require("capa.read")` (SYSTEM enforce) to `get_current_user` + a new **`_readable_capas`** per-row filter (`gather_grants("capa.read")` once → keep rows where `authorize(grants, "capa.read", _process_scope(row[0].process_id), ctx).allow`; **filter-not-403**, SYSTEM grants match every row byte-identical, `source_ip` threaded; a process-less ad-hoc CAPA needs a SYSTEM grant); (6) `GET /capas/{id}` + `/approval` moved to **`_capa_read_scoped = require("capa.read", async_scope_resolver=_capa_scope)`** (PROCESS-enforce; a SYSTEM Top-Management approver still matches; a bound owner reads their own process's CAPA; else 403). **Load-bearing decisions:** (a) **the button gate keys on `capa.create`, NEVER on "has readable processes"** — an Internal Auditor holds SYSTEM `process.read` (a non-empty process list) + SYSTEM `capa.read` (board access) but no `capa.create` at any scope, so gating on process-count would leak the button to auditors (and regress today's behaviour); the per-process `capa.create` probe is the only correct discriminator. (b) **The button gate is O(1)** — the owner chose `SYSTEM ‖ first-readable-process probe` over a `useQueries` fan-out across ALL readable processes, specifically to avoid firing N `/me/permissions` calls for every broad-`process.read` caller (an auditor → all org processes) on each board load; the owner-assignment binding mints `process.read` + `capa.create` over the SAME bound set, so for a single-role bound owner every readable process is a createable one (first is representative). (c) **filter-not-403 on `GET /capas`** (doc 18 §5.2, matching records/search/processes) — a no-grant caller now gets `200`+empty (an empty board) instead of the no-access panel; the FE's defensive 403 handling is retained but effectively dead for `/capas`. **⚠ THE structural lesson — the FE-only premise was incomplete: a feature cannot reach its target persona if the board's own LIST read is SYSTEM-gated.** The deferral note + the brief both fixated on the `POST /capas` write (already process-scoped) + the button, and MISSED that `GET /capas` (the board's render gate) was the real chokepoint — Codex P1 caught it; the fix is the same read-filter S-process-scope-2 applied to `/processes`. **⚠ Other traps a future session MUST carry:** (1) **the ruff PostToolUse hook strips just-added imports → body-first** (hit again: `RequestContext`/`authorize`/`gather_grants` were stripped because the body using them came in a later edit). (2) **Mantine's `required` label appends an asterisk to the accessible name** (`getByLabelText("Process")` exact FAILS for a required field → "Process *") — query the required picker by its requireProcess-only PLACEHOLDER ("Pick the owning process"), a genuine discriminator, not the tautological `/^Process/` (which matches both labels) or an exact "(optional)" negative (always null for a required field — the web-test-trap MAJOR was exactly this false-PASS). (3) **the board's "＋ Raise CAPA" open button AND the modal's "Raise CAPA" submit both match `/Raise CAPA/`** while the modal is open → target the modal submit by EXACT `{name:"Raise CAPA"}`; and the board's Severity FILTER (`aria-label="Severity"`) clashes with the modal's Severity while open → scope or avoid. (4) **a backend read-port that flips a list to filter-not-403 must update any PRE-EXISTING test asserting the old 403** (the diff-critic CRITICAL: `test_capa.py::test_capa_read_requires_grant` still asserted `GET /capas`→403; renamed → `test_capa_list_filters_not_403_ncr_still_enforces`, 200+empty while `/ncrs` stays SYSTEM-403). **Named deferrals (not faked):** (i) **the multi-role first-process-probe false-negative** — a caller who is BOTH an auditor (SYSTEM `process.read` → all processes) AND a bound owner of a *non-first* process may have the button hidden (a SAFE false-negative — never a crash/over-grant; server-403 is the boundary); the O(1) probe is the owner-approved trade-off, with a future backend `can_raise` signal the fix if it ever becomes real (Codex re-raised this as a fresh P2, declined-with-rationale). (ii) **NCR raise NOT widened** — `ncr.create` is in the catalog but bound to NO role (SYSTEM-override-only in v1), so no Process-Owner can hold it at any scope → the NCR raise gap is not exercisable by a bound owner (owner-confirmed; the picker would be dead). **Test delta: +5 api integration** (`test_capa_process_scope.py`, CI-only on Windows: list-narrows-to-owned [P1/P2/ad-hoc] · detail+approval-enforce-process-scope [owned 200 / unowned 403] · a SYSTEM `capa.read` holder reads a PROCESS-scoped CAPA [the Top-Mgmt path] · no-grant-list-is-200-empty-not-403 · a bound owner raises ONLY in an owned process [own 201, process-less 403, unowned-P2 403]) **+ the `test_capa.py` 403→200-empty rename/fix; +7 web** (`CapaBoardPage.test.tsx`: button shows for a PROCESS-only `capa.create` holder · hidden for a read-only auditor · the board threads `requireProcess` [placeholder discriminator] · reopen discards the draft; `RaiseCapaModal.test.tsx`: submits the chosen `process_id` · omits the picker when no processes · `requireProcess` gates submit until a pick); **web 1086→1093** (fixtures pinned via `satisfies MePermissions`/`CapaRaiseBody`). ruff + ruff-format + mypy-strict clean (modulo the Windows `O_NOFOLLOW` baseline); redocly clean; CI 11/11 green (all 4 integration shards). **diff-critic CRITICAL** (the stale `GET /capas`→403 assertion) **→ fixed**; **web-test-trap-reviewer MAJOR** (the board required-picker test was a false-PASS — tautological label queries) **→ fixed** (placeholder discriminator + the modal's real submit-gate), plus 2 MINORs (modal-test tautology, persistent-modal reset → conditional mount). **Codex** P1 (board read-filter) + P2 (required picker) FIXED, the fresh multi-role P2 declined-with-rationale; all 3 threads resolved. **Merge gate:** green CI + 0 unresolved threads + reviewers' findings fixed + owner's explicit go. PR [#220](https://github.com/CoJoA13/EasySynQ/pull/220), squash `6e645f8`.

## v1.x RECORDS PROCESS-SCOPE — Process-Owner record read + authoring + the clause-map grant (the named S-process-scope-1 follow-up; 0057)

> The deferred "records process-scope read" follow-up S-process-scope-1 named after its records read enrichment did NOT converge under Codex and was reverted. Specced first, then shipped in sequenced slices: **spec → R (read) → W (write-enable) → C (clauseMap.read grant)**. The structural insight (from the S-process-scope-1 revert): a READ surface is only safe to scope by process bindings if EVERY write path that CREATES those bindings re-authorizes the target — so the read DECOUPLES from the write (R ships safely with the write gates process-blind), and write-enable (W) is its own sequenced slice with per-target re-auth. The whole records WRITE re-auth is the non-converging surface (Codex took 3–4 rounds each on R + W); **trim-don't-chase** is what converged it.

- **S-records-C** (Process Owner gains `clauseMap.read` @ SYSTEM — the create-in-process wizard's clause step) — **the LAST functional piece of the arc.** A bound Process-Owner (the seeded "Process Owner" role assigned with a PROCESS `bound_scope`, as owner-assignment mints) got **403** on `GET /clauses` (gated `clauseMap.read`, SYSTEM-scoped) → the wizard's clause step was empty. **What shipped (migration `0057`, NO new permission key — `clauseMap.read` already exists [`0004`]; catalog stays 102; R47 = an R38-ADDITIVE role GRANT, not a key):** (1) **migration 0057** grants the Process Owner role a SYSTEM-scoped `clauseMap.read` `role_grant`, inserted for EVERY org's Process Owner role (by role NAME — not the `DEFAULT` org `0004` targets — so it reaches a renamed install like `ORG_EXAMPLE`; idempotent `on_conflict_do_nothing` on `(org_id,role_id,permission_id)`; data-only → `alembic check` unaffected). (2) **THE coupled resolver fix** in `services/authz/repository._grant_from_role`: the grant alone is INSUFFICIENT — a bound owner's `role_assignment` carries a PROCESS `bound_scope`, and the resolver did `raw = bound_scope or scope_template` (bound_scope REPLACES every grant's scope), so the new SYSTEM `clauseMap.read` got CLAMPED to the bound PROCESS → unsatisfiable against the SYSTEM clause-map resource (`pdp._matches_scope` PROCESS = `bool(scoped & resource.process_ids)`, and a SYSTEM resource has empty `process_ids`). Fix: an **EXPLICIT `level=="SYSTEM"` template is never clamped by a bound_scope** (a SYSTEM-finest grant has no `:assignment_process` placeholder to concretize); every parameterized (PROCESS/FOLDER/…) template still defers to the bound_scope. **⚠ Traps a future session MUST carry:** (1) **`finest_scope` is documentary, but the bound_scope CLAMP is the real blocker** — unlike S-process-scope-2 (where threading `process_ids` was the whole fix), here the SYSTEM grant exists but the bound_scope replace-semantics ate it; the resolver fix is the load-bearing half, not the migration. (2) **blast radius verified = exactly this case** — every pre-existing bound assignment uses `bound_scope={"level":"SYSTEM"}` (a SYSTEM bound + any template = SYSTEM either way → unchanged), and the only PROCESS-bound role today (owner-assignment Process Owner) had NO SYSTEM grant before this slice; no existing SYSTEM grant is widened. (3) **exempt only an EXPLICIT SYSTEM template, not `_scope_from_dict`'s SYSTEM default** — a NULL/level-less template defers to the bound_scope (the conservative, non-widening direction); pre-empts the reviewers' NULL-template note. (4) the ruff hook strips just-added imports (body-first; hit twice here). **Test delta: +1 api unit** (`test_authz_resolver.py`, 5 cases — SYSTEM-not-clamped, PROCESS-still-concretized, SYSTEM-bound-unchanged, no-bound-stays-SYSTEM, level-less-defers; runs LOCALLY) **+1 api integration** (`test_clauses.py::test_bound_process_owner_reads_clauses` — a bound owner reads `/clauses` → 200; discriminates BOTH the migration grant AND the resolver fix) **+ a Process-Owner grant assertion in `test_authz`** (catalog stays 102, the grant is SYSTEM-scoped); **web unchanged.** ruff + format + mypy-strict clean; migration round-trip up↔down↔`alembic check` CLEAN on PG16 (grant verified `Process Owner/clauseMap.read/SYSTEM`, catalog 102). **migration-reviewer + diff-critic both CLEAN** (the NULL-template note pre-empted via the explicit-level exemption); **Codex CLEAN** ("Didn't find any major issues"). **Merge gate:** green CI (11/11) + 0 unresolved threads + reviewers + Codex clean + owner's plan approval (pre-authorized the merge path). PR [#218](https://github.com/CoJoA13/EasySynQ/pull/218), squash `71ecfcf`.

- **S-records-W** (Process-Owner record AUTHORING — write-enable + per-target re-auth) — **enables a bound Process-Owner to AUTHOR records bound to their process, the write half R decoupled from.** **What shipped (NO migration [head stays `0056`], NO new key; contract = gate-note text):** `_record_read_scope` renamed `_record_process_scope`, now resolving BOTH the `_read` AND the per-record `_create_scoped` (write-enable) gates; a new **`_enforce_target_process_record`** (the `documents._enforce_target_process` analogue — re-enforce `record.create` over a scope whose `process_ids` is JUST the target, so a SYSTEM/ARTIFACT/FOLDER holder still passes but a PROCESS holder must own the SPECIFIC target). Re-auth points: **capture** = a per-process loop over the source doc's processes (own ALL; mirrors `documents.create`); **evidence-link add/remove** = re-auth a PROCESS target the caller owns, while EVERY other target type (CLAUSE/DOCUMENT/FINDING/CAPA_STAGE) re-auths over an EMPTY set → broad/SYSTEM holders only (the TRIM — satisfies the spec's R3-2 capa_stage gap by DENY, not by scoping); **correction** = re-auth the UNION of the successor's new-source processes AND the original's FULL effective binding, per-process. `_dispose` stays process-blind. **⚠ THE non-convergence (this surface) — diff-critic CRITICAL + 3 substantive Codex rounds, trim-don't-chase converged it:** the diff-critic caught the **capture untightened** (the base intersection-match passes if ANY source process is owned → a P1-owner could mint a P1+P2 record under a shared doc) → the per-process loop. Codex then surfaced a NEW correction/evidence-link edge each round: **round-4 P1** — the correction re-auth used `source_processes OR effective(original)`, which SHORT-CIRCUITED on an owned source process and skipped an unowned EXISTING binding (a P1-owner could supersede a record co-bound to P2; `capture_correction` does NOT carry the original's EvidenceForLinks → a P1-only successor P2 owners lose) → **replaced the `or` with a UNION** (own every process the original is currently bound to + any new source); **round-5 P2** — a TOCTOU: the correction read the binding BEFORE `capture_correction`'s FOR UPDATE lock, and `link_evidence` inserted without locking the Record → **lock the Record row FOR UPDATE in correction_endpoint (held through the supersede, `populate_existing`), link_evidence, AND unlink_evidence** so all three binding-relevant writers serialize on the one row; **round-6** (2 deeper P2 TOCTOUs — re-auth-under-lock for `link_evidence`, lock the unbounded `correction_of` ancestor chain — + the CAPA-stage residual re-raised) were **TRIMMED as bounded named residuals** (single-org, deep interleavings; the deterministic escalation + same-record TOCTOU are already closed; fixes are unbounded/invasive on a non-converging surface). **⚠ Traps a future session MUST carry:** (1) **a READ surface is only safe to scope by process bindings if EVERY binding-MINTING write re-authorizes the target** — capture (per-process), evidence-link (PROCESS-target), correction (union of the original's full binding); the `_enforce_target_process_record` discipline for EVERY target type. (2) **the records WRITE re-auth does NOT converge — trim, don't chase**: when a surface keeps generating edges round after round, TRIM the over-grant to broad-only/deny (evidence-link non-PROCESS targets) and the FOR UPDATE lock is the STRUCTURAL terminal fix, not another logic patch that invites the next round. (3) the final-state of a correction-vs-link race can't DISTINGUISH the bypass from the legal correction-wins case → prove the lock with a deterministic 2-session held-lock-blocks test, not an `asyncio.gather` final-state assertion. (4) `populate_existing` on the correction's locking load is LOAD-BEARING (the handler reads `original.source_document_id` off the locked object). (5) the ruff hook strips just-added imports (body-first, recurring). **Named residual: `POST /records:init-upload` stays SYSTEM-gated** (process-less byte staging → a Process-Owner authors FORM records + PROCESS links against an already-staged blob, but staging a NEW evidence blob is a bounded follow-up); plus the trimmed Process-Owner CAPA/FINDING/DOCUMENT evidence (broad-only) + the 2 round-6 TOCTOUs. **Test delta: +8 api integration** (`test_records_process_scope.py`: capture-in-owned-process, evidence-link target re-auth add/remove, artifact-holder-not-over-blocked, cannot-introduce-unowned-source, cannot-capture-under-shared-doc, cannot-link-non-process-target, correction-reauths-inherited-binding, correction-cannot-supersede-co-bound [round-4], binding-writes-serialize-on-record-lock [round-5]); **web unchanged.** ruff + format + mypy-strict clean; **diff-critic ×3 (CRITICAL on capture → CLEAN on the union + lock fixes); Codex 3 substantive rounds (4/5/6).** **Merge gate:** green CI (11/11) + 0 unresolved threads (all rounds replied + resolved, round-6 trimmed with rationale) + owner's explicit per-round go. PR [#217](https://github.com/CoJoA13/EasySynQ/pull/217), squash `6dcb976`.

- **S-records-R** (records process-scope READ via a DECOUPLED read-only resolver) — **a bound Process-Owner's PROCESS-scoped `record.read` authorizes records bound to their owned processes, WITHOUT enabling them to mint a binding (the writes stay process-blind; authoring is W).** The decoupling is the spec's central result: a read-only resolver ships safely with NO write re-auth because no NARROW holder can mint a binding (every binding-minting write 403s them at the process-blind base gate). **What shipped (NO migration [head stays `0056`], NO new key — rides `record.read`, `finest_scope` documentary; no contract change):** (1) `services/records/repository.py` = the ONE source of truth — **`record_process_ids`** (leg A `EvidenceForLink(PROCESS)` targets ∪ leg B source-doc `ProcessLink`s), **`record_process_ids_effective`** (+ the R3-1 `correction_of` walk — ITERATIVE, visited-set, no hop cap), and a batched **`record_process_ids_for`** for the list; (2) a new **`_record_read_scope`** resolver (full context + the process binding) for the `_read` gate ONLY — `_create_scoped`/`_dispose` stay on the process-BLIND `_record_scope` (the decoupling); the `GET /records` list row-filter enriched (batched binding + per-row R3-1 fallback); (3) `packs/service.classify_candidates` refactored onto `records_repo.record_process_ids_effective` (the pack classifier + `/records` now agree from one source; the duplicate left `packs/repository`). **3 Codex rounds:** CX-3 hop-cap → **visited set** (cycle-safe, no cap that drops a long legitimate correction chain) + a 2-hop test; the pack-candidate query must ALSO walk corrections (so the pack `record.read` exposure matches the records gate); the candidate "owned" predicate must equal a non-empty own union EXACTLY. **⚠ Traps:** the decoupling (read resolver separate, writes process-blind) is WHY R is safe without W's re-auth — never fold the write gates onto the process-aware resolver without W's `_enforce_target_process_record`. **Test delta: +6 api integration** (`test_records_process_scope.py`: reads-owned/403s-unowned, list-filters, source-less-invisible, R3-1 single + 2-hop correction-chain visibility, + the read/write decoupling invariant — reads 200 but a per-record write 403s); **web unchanged.** ruff + mypy-strict clean; **diff-critic CLEAN (prompted the decoupling-invariant coverage add); Codex 3 rounds.** **Merge gate:** green CI + 0 unresolved threads + Codex clean + owner's go. PR [#216](https://github.com/CoJoA13/EasySynQ/pull/216), squash `1fc8f24`.

- **S-records-spec** (the authz model + sequenced slice plan for "records process-scope read") — **the deferred follow-up, SPECCED + adversarially validated BEFORE code** (the surface that did not converge in S-process-scope-1). `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-19-records-process-scope-authz-design.md`: the model + a **4-lens refutation panel** (read-only-safe, 0 holes). **Central result:** enriching a DECOUPLED read-only resolver (read + list only, write gates process-blind) is escalation-free with NO write re-auth — a Process-Owner reads their records but mints nothing (every binding-minting write 403s them at the base gate; capture inherits only the source-doc's already-re-authorized links; the minter enumeration is complete). The write re-auth (the B-work) is needed ONLY to ENABLE Process-Owner authoring, which alone opens the `capa_stage` target class. **Ratified plan: Slice R (read) + Slice W (write-enable, sequenced) + Slice C (`clauseMap.read` → Process-Owner bundle, R38).** Four bounded caveats named as accepted residuals (capture doc-spans-processes visibility, sibling SYSTEM-gated read surfaces, the `correction_of` hop cap, AZ-INV-8 monotonicity). Docs-only. PR [#215](https://github.com/CoJoA13/EasySynQ/pull/215), squash `4e929a9`.

## v1.x OWNER-ASSIGNMENT — PROCESS-scoped RBAC binding: substitutes the long-deferred `:assignment_process` placeholder (0056)

- **S-process-scope-2** (per-process read-filter on `GET /processes` + `/map`) — **"Slice A" of the named S-process-scope-1 "records process-scope read" follow-up: the smallest, lowest-risk, highest-unblock piece, split out from the deeper records work.** A bound Process-Owner's `process.read` is PROCESS-scoped, but the process-landscape reads were gated `require("process.read")` at **default SYSTEM scope** → a bound owner got a hard **403**, so the create-in-process wizard PICKER (shipped #211) was empty for them. **What shipped (BE-only, NO migration [head stays `0056`], NO new permission key [rides `process.read`, catalog stays 102]; contract = description text only — no shape change):** (1) **`GET /processes` + `GET /processes/map`** moved from `Depends(_read)` (SYSTEM enforce) to `Depends(get_current_user)` + a new `_readable_processes()` **row-filter** (`gather_grants("process.read")` once → per-row `authorize` over `ResourceContext(process_ids={str(p.id)})`; the `GET /records` + `/search` precedent). `/map` also **drops any edge whose endpoint is hidden** (no dangling edge to an unreadable process). (2) **`GET /processes/{id}`** moved `_read` → the existing `_read_scoped` (enforce `process.read` at the process's PROCESS scope; still **403-on-deny**, single-resource). (3) the now-unused `_read` (SYSTEM) removed. **THE load-bearing correction (vs the brief, which assumed a migration/finest_scope change):** **no migration and no new key are needed** — the seeded *Process Owner* bundle ALREADY grants `record.read`/`process.read` at PROCESS scope (`0004_seed_authz.py:205,322`), and **`finest_scope` is documentary catalog metadata, NEVER enforced at runtime** (`pdp.py:81-83` matches a PROCESS grant purely on `bool(scoped & resource.process_ids)`); the only blocker was the empty `resource.process_ids` on the read gate. **Decisions/behavior:** a SYSTEM grant matches every row (`_matches_scope` returns True for SYSTEM regardless of `process_ids`) → QMS Owner / Internal Auditor / a `demo` SYSTEM override see the org-wide landscape **byte-identical**; the **one observable change (owner-approved): a no-grant caller on the LIST/MAP now gets `200` + empty instead of `403`** — the documented list-surface **filter-not-403** doctrine (doc 18 §5.2 / doc 15 §9.3), matching records/search (the list/map GETs no longer document a `403`). `GET /clauses` was explicitly **OUT** (a per-process clause filter is ill-defined — clauses are framework-anchored reference data; `clauseMap.read` isn't even in the Process-Owner bundle) → a separate owner decision. **Understand phase** ran a 6-reader → synthesis Workflow that produced the A/B1/B2/B3 split (B1 write-path re-auth BEFORE B2 read scoping is the structural insight from the S-process-scope-1 records revert) and surfaced the no-migration correction. **Codex round 1 — 3 P2, triaged 2-fixed/1-declined (a clean diff-critic passed):** **CX-1** (thread `source_ip`) **FIXED** — `_readable_processes` now threads `request.client.host` so an `ip_allow` grant evaluates exactly as the replaced `require()` enforce did (v1-deferred but kept faithful; the S-leadership-cx1 precedent); **CX-2** (a row-filtered child row leaks its hidden parent's id via `parent_id`) **FIXED** — `_process(p, visible_ids)` nulls `parent_id` on the list/map when the parent isn't visible (the edge-filter rationale; detail/authoring keep it); **CX-3** (honour `include_subprocesses`) **DECLINED** — `include_subprocesses` (docs/07 §5.3, "default true") is documented but **UNIMPLEMENTED anywhere in the v1 PDP** (the PROCESS match is a pure own-id intersection, no resolver walks `parent_id`, owner-assignment mints explicit `process_ids`, `_document_scope`/records/search all use own-id/own-link sets) → a cross-cutting authz-model change that must land consistently across documents/records/processes, named as its own follow-up. **⚠ Traps a future session MUST carry:** (1) **for a process landscape read, `finest_scope` is a red herring — it is documentary, not a runtime gate**; the grant already existed, so the fix was purely threading `process_ids` into the resource context. (2) **a row-filter must sanitize EVERY pointer to a hidden row, not just the rows themselves** — the `/map` edge filter AND the `parent_id` null (CX-2) are the same "no dangling pointer / no hidden-id leak" rule. (3) **the ruff PostToolUse hook strips just-added imports → body-first** (recurring; hit again here). (4) **Slice A converged in ONE Codex round** (the predicted LOW risk) precisely because it's a pure READ with no write-path-minted visibility channel — the opposite of the records read (B2) that didn't converge; the A/B split exists so the safe read ships first. **Deferred (named, not faked) — the rest of "records process-scope read":** **B1** (records evidence-link POST/DELETE + correction RE-AUTHORIZE the target/effective-source process — the `_enforce_target_process` analogue, the safety floor) → **B2** (scope records read by a single shared loader == `packs::record_process_ids`, only after B1) → **B3** (the pre-existing `capa_stage` evidence-link authz gap, orthogonal); plus the `GET /clauses` fix (add `clauseMap.read` SYSTEM to the bundle vs route the wizard's clause step elsewhere — an owner call) and the NEW **subprocess/descendant-inclusion** authz follow-up surfaced by CX-3. **Test delta: +2 api integration** (`test_process_scope.py`: a bound owner of P1-not-P2 narrows on list/map with the P1→P2 edge dropped + a SYSTEM-author full-landscape baseline; a child-owner's `parent_id` nulled while the SYSTEM author sees the real linkage) **+1 assertion + 1 rename in `test_processes.py`** (`test_read_requires_permission` → `test_list_filters_not_403_for_no_grant`, the `200`+empty semantic + detail-still-403; + a SYSTEM-holder `404`-not-`403` detail assertion); **web unchanged** (the FE `useProcesses()` already degrades-on-403 and renders whatever it gets → the picker now populates for a bound owner with no FE change). ruff + ruff-format + mypy-strict clean (modulo the Windows `O_NOFOLLOW` baseline); redocly clean; CI 11/11 green (all 4 integration shards). **diff-critic CLEAN** (SYSTEM-byte-identical, the /map dangling-edge filter, no write-path over-grant channel, the 403-before-404 detail order, the dropped enforce-audit matching records/search, the tests' genuineness all verified — it also prompted the SYSTEM-404 coverage add). **Merge gate:** green CI + 0 unresolved threads (3 Codex P2 — 2 fixed, 1 declined-with-rationale — all replied + resolved) + Codex 👍 re-approve + owner's explicit go. PR [#213](https://github.com/CoJoA13/EasySynQ/pull/213), squash `e346239`.

- **S-process-scope-1** (enrich the secondary auth surfaces with `process_ids` + the `document.create` link-on-create write-path) — **the named S-owner-assignment-1 follow-up "enrich every auth surface with `process_ids`", shipped for the surfaces that CONVERGED.** S-owner-assignment-1 migrated `_document_scope` (document detail/list/edit), but the workflow approval-history reads, the search row-filter, and `document.create` all built their own `ResourceContext` WITHOUT `process_ids`, so a bound Process-Owner's PROCESS-scoped grant mis-denied them. **What shipped (BE+FE, NO migration [head stays `0056`], NO new permission key [catalog stays 102, R38 not engaged]; contract = ONE additive `DocumentCreate.process_ids`):** (1) a shared `vault_repo.process_ids_for_doc(s)` loader — `_document_scope_by_id` + the `GET /documents` list batch refactored onto it (parity-preserving, deletes the two duplicated inline ProcessLink queries); (2) **workflow** — `GET /workflow-instances/{id}` + `GET /documents/{id}/approval` carry the subject doc's `process_ids` (both gate `document.read`); (3) **search** — `_readable_hits` + `/search/suggest` batch-load `process_ids` per candidate hit; (4) **`document.create` write-path** — `DocumentCreate.process_ids` (optional, `maxItems:50`) links the new doc AT creation: `create_document(processes=...)` creates the doc + `ProcessLink` rows + `PROCESS_LINKED` audit (with `identifier=doc.identifier` → `to_audit_event` maps `scope_ref=identifier`, byte-compatible with the standalone link endpoint's per-document trail) in ONE txn/commit (atomic — a failed link can't strand a doc its process-scoped creator could no longer read); authorize `document.create` over the declared processes THEN re-`enforce` `document.manage_metadata` PER declared process (mirrors `_enforce_target_process`, so an owner of P1 can't smuggle a link to an unowned P2 — the S-owner-assignment-1 escalation class), empty `process_ids` ⇒ byte-identical to the pre-slice create; plus a processes `MultiSelect` on the New-Document wizard. **Load-bearing decisions:** the enrichment is **strictly monotonic / AZ-INV-8-safe** — the PDP matches a PROCESS grant iff `selector.process_ids ∩ resource.process_ids ≠ ∅`, so adding `process_ids` only newly *matches* a grant already targeting that exact process, never widens SYSTEM/ARTIFACT/FOLDER/DOC_CLASS (re-derived per surface). `_decision_scope` (the workflow approve/review DECIDE gate) was deliberately EXCLUDED — the seeded "Process Owner" role holds no `document.approve`/`review`, so enriching it is a dead no-op (named, not missed). **⚠ THE big lesson — the records read enrichment did NOT converge and was TRIMMED OUT mid-slice:** records read (a record inherits its source-doc + `EvidenceForLink(PROCESS)` scope, matching the packs `record.read` gate) was built + reviewed, but Codex surfaced a NEW evidence-link/correction over-grant edge every round — **CX-1** (evidence-link POST doesn't re-auth the PROCESS target → an owner of P1 makes a record readable to P2's owners), **CX-4** (correction doesn't re-scope its source), **R2-1** (the re-auth must PRESERVE the record scope, replacing only `process_ids`, else ARTIFACT/FOLDER holders over-blocked), **R2-2** (the evidence-link DELETE also needs target re-auth), **R2-3** (`capture_correction` FORCES a source-backed original's OWN source — the body's is ignored — so re-auth the EFFECTIVE source), **R3-1** (a source-less evidence-linked record's correction loses its process binding → invisible to its creator), **R3-2** (a PRE-EXISTING `capa_stage` evidence-link authz gap the partial re-auth highlights). After 3 substantive rounds with no convergence, the owner's call was to **revert the records read enrichment** (restore `api/records.py`, `services/records/repository.py`, `services/packs/repository.py` to `main`) and ship only the converged surfaces. **⚠ Traps a future session MUST carry:** (1) **adding `process_ids` to a READ surface is monotonic and safe, but adding it to a surface whose process bindings are created by UN-RE-AUTHORIZED write paths (records evidence-link / correction) opens an over-grant** — the read enrichment is only as sound as the write-path target re-authorization (the `_enforce_target_process` discipline) for EVERY target type, AND the correction-chain scope; that whole sub-project is deferred. (2) the `document.create` chicken-and-egg (a new doc has no ProcessLink) is the reason create needs `process_ids` in the BODY + atomic link-on-create — it can't be a pure resolver enrichment like the reads. (3) the ruff PostToolUse hook strips just-added imports (body-first, recurring). (4) **Codex authz non-convergence again** — the shipped surfaces (workflow/search/create) had ZERO findings across 4 rounds; 100% of findings were on records (reverted) or the deferred FE; triage per round, and when a surface keeps generating edges, TRIM it rather than chase. **Deferred (named, not faked) — ONE focused follow-up, "records process-scope read":** the records read enrichment bundled with its write-path target re-auth (evidence-link POST/DELETE + correction effective-source + EVERY non-process evidence-link target incl. the pre-existing `capa_stage` gap), the correction-chain scope, the pre-existing packs `record.read` exposure, AND **per-process read filtering on `GET /processes` + `GET /clauses`** (both SYSTEM-gated today → the create-in-process wizard works for SYSTEM/Author authors but the picker + clause step are empty for a bound Process-Owner; the `document.create` backend is correct regardless). Codex **CX-3** (require `document.create` per declared process) was **DECLINED** — the end-state (a doc linked to P1+P2) is reachable with the same perms via `create([P1])` + the standalone `link(P2)` (which gates only `manage_metadata`-for-P2), so per-process create would be stricter than the documented decomposed flow. **Test delta: +8 api integration** (`test_process_scope.py`, CI-only on Windows: create-in-owned-process + multi-process dedup + can't-create-unlinked + the P1+P2 escalation-403 + unknown-process-422 + create-holder-without-manage_metadata + the workflow approval/instance 403→200 flip on a linked doc with the unlinked-stays-403 boundary + search-sees-only-linked-effective) **+2 web** (`NewDocumentWizard.test.tsx`: the picker sends `process_ids`; the omitted-when-empty byte-identical body); **web 1084→1086**, no api unit added. ruff + mypy-strict clean (modulo the Windows `O_NOFOLLOW` baseline); redocly clean; full web `1086/1086`. **diff-critic (×2) + web-test-trap-reviewer + a 3-lens review Workflow (missed-surface / authz-edges / create-parity) all CLEAN on the shipped surfaces**; the records findings were 100% Codex (the documented authz-non-convergence pattern). **Merge gate:** green CI (11/11) + 0 unresolved threads (12 total — records moot/reverted, CX-3 declined, FE deferred — all replied + resolved) + owner's explicit go. PR [#211](https://github.com/CoJoA13/EasySynQ/pull/211), squash `42f8de6`.

- **S-owner-assignment-1** (real process-owner binding + the repo-wide `_document_scope`→`process_ids` migration) — **closes the owner-assignment gap that every prior PROCESS-scoped slice rode around via SYSTEM overrides.** The whole PROCESS-scoped ABAC engine was already built (`ScopeLevel.PROCESS`, the PDP `_matches_scope` process_ids intersection, `role_assignment.bound_scope` as the concretization seam, the seeded "Process Owner" permission-role), but nothing turned "Diego owns Purchasing" into a concrete `process_id`: every seeded Process-Owner grant carried the literal placeholder `selector.process_id = ":assignment_process"` (migrations 0004/0036/0040/0043/0044/0052) that matches no real process, so PROCESS-finest-scope authority was satisfied only by SYSTEM overrides. **What shipped (BE+FE, migration `0056`, NO new permission key — rides the seeded-but-unenforced `process.assign_owner`; catalog stays 102, R38 not engaged):** (1) the ERD-deferred **`org_role_assignment`** RACI carrier (`org_role_id`→`org_role`, `user_id`→`app_user`, `process_id` NULLABLE→`process`, `UNIQUE(org_role_id,user_id,process_id)`) + its ORM (registered in `db/models/__init__.py`) + two additive `event_type` values `PROCESS_OWNER_ASSIGNED`/`_REVOKED` (autocommit_block ADD VALUE; NO new `audit_object_type` — events key on the existing `process` value); (2) **`services/owner_assignment.py`** — `assign_process_owner`/`revoke_process_owner` in ONE txn: resolve-or-create the single generic "Process Owner" org_role, upsert the org_role_assignment RACI row, and **mint/extend a SINGLE PROCESS-scoped Process-Owner `role_assignment.bound_scope`** carrying a GROWING `process_ids` set (the PDP reads `selector.process_id OR process_ids`, so one row authorizes many owned processes — `role_assignment` has no uniqueness backstop, so one growing row beats N rows), **system-attributed** (the `process.assign_owner` content-tier gate is the sole authority; NOT re-gated through SYSTEM-tier `permission.grant`), serialized on the parent `app_user` row FOR UPDATE, audited both halves, then `invalidate_user_permissions`; (3) `POST`/`DELETE /processes/{id}/owner` + `GET /processes/{id}/owners` (+ openapi); (4) the **repo-wide `_document_scope` migration** — `_document_scope_by_id` now folds the doc's `ProcessLink` ids into `ResourceContext.process_ids` (deleting the single-use `_document_scope_with_processes`, repointing `_distribute`), and the `GET /documents` list read-filter batch-loads process links per row (no N+1), so a bound Process-Owner's `document.*` grant authorizes across EVERY document gate, not just `distribute`; (5) a minimal **`admin/ProcessesAdmin.tsx`** tab (assign/revoke owners; coarse SYSTEM `process.assign_owner` probe gates the write affordances, server 403 is the true boundary). **THE central design device — the `managed_by:"owner_assignment"` bound_scope marker** (the PDP `_scope_from_dict` ignores it — reads level/selector/predicates only): owner-assignment-minted grants confer the scoped PERMISSION set but are EXCLUDED from every ROLE-MEMBERSHIP consumer. **⚠ THE load-bearing lesson — "mint a Process-Owner `role_assignment`" leaks role MEMBERSHIP everywhere, and Codex (3 substantive rounds, 6→6→3 findings) is the only finder of it** (clean diff-critic/migration-reviewer/web-test-trap passed the core code each round): the marker exclusion had to be applied to (a) **workflow candidate resolution** `wf_repo.users_with_roles` — the **P1**: migration 0043's DCR-MAJOR-approval stage NAMES "Process Owner" as a candidate role and `users_with_roles` joins on `Role.name` (scope-blind), so an owner of process A would otherwise become a candidate for every "Process Owner"-gated stage org-wide (a DCR for process B); (b) **ack audience** `resolve_audience` — same flood onto every process owner for a role-targeted distribution; (c) the **generic role surface** `authz.py` — `list_user_roles` HIDES marked rows + `revoke_user_role` 409s them (a bare delete would orphan the org_role_assignment RACI row), and the marker is **un-forgeable** (`assign_user_role` strips a caller-supplied `managed_by`). **⚠ The `_document_scope` widening ALSO introduced a process-link ESCALATION** — `document.manage_metadata` now matches via ANY linked process, so an owner of A on a doc linked to A+B could mutate links to B → the link/unlink endpoints re-`enforce` `document.manage_metadata` against the TARGET process scope (`_enforce_target_process`: SYSTEM/ARTIFACT/FOLDER holders still pass, a PROCESS owner must own the target). **⚠ Other traps:** an idempotent re-assign must SKIP the phantom `PROCESS_OWNER_ASSIGNED`/`ROLE_ASSIGN` audit rows (track whether the binding or the process_ids actually changed); a narrowing revoke emits `ROLE_REVOKE` (not `ROLE_ASSIGN` — revoke always reduces access); `GET /owners` is process-scoped read (a PROCESS owner can read their own process's owners — **the FIRST CI failure was a TEST bug**: it's `process.read`-gated and the tests granted only `assign_owner` → 403, then the next test iterated the 403 error body → `TypeError`); the optional `org_role_id` body field was REMOVED (every binding keys to ONE generic org_role → at most one org_role_assignment per (user,process) → revoke can't over-delete an unrelated RACI binding); `bound_scope` is JSONB → reassign a fresh dict, never in-place (S-ing-4); FK names match the Base naming convention (alembic-check phantom-DROP) + the ORM registered in `__init__.py`; the ruff hook strips just-added imports (body-first). **Deferred (named, not faked) — ONE coherent follow-up, "enrich every auth surface with `process_ids`":** the slice migrated `_document_scope` (document detail/list/edit/approve work), but `document.create` (POST /documents authorizes from folder/doc_class), workflow approval-history reads (`/workflow-instances/{id}`, `/documents/{id}/approval`), the `search` row-filter, and `record.read`/list scopes all build their OWN ResourceContext without process_ids — so the minted Process-Owner grant is **read/edit/approve-effective on EXISTING process-linked documents** but create + those secondary reads need the same enrichment (each a sibling resolver). Plus the FE per-process assign-owner probe (deliberate coarse-SYSTEM gate; additive if process-scoped `assign_owner` delegation lands); the **CAPA Raise process-scope deferral is now UNBLOCKED** (the binding exists) but the Raise process-picker is still unwired. **Test delta: +11 api integration** (CI-only on Windows: the 403→200 doc-read flip on a process-linked doc · the union + idempotent no-op · revoke-narrows-then-drops · admin SYSTEM + PROCESS grants survive · workflow-candidacy exclusion · managed-revoke 409 · target-process link authority · forged-marker strip) **+4 web** (`ProcessesAdmin.test.tsx`: roster + read-only drawer, assign, remove, calm 403); **web 1080→1084.** No api unit added. ruff + mypy-strict clean (modulo the Windows `O_NOFOLLOW` baseline); migration round-trip up↔down↔`alembic check` CLEAN on PG16; redocly clean. **diff-critic 1 MINOR** (owner-revoke could clobber a separately-granted admin Process-Owner row → the `managed_by` marker; fixed + tested); **migration-reviewer + web-test-trap-reviewer CLEAN; Codex 3 substantive rounds → round 4 CLEAN 👍** (the design class genuinely doesn't converge — each round surfaced a new role-membership/scope-widening interaction; triage per round: fix the introduced issue, defer bounded-safe). **Merge gate:** green CI (11/11) + 0 unresolved threads (15 total, all replied + resolved) + Codex clean + owner's explicit go. ## v1.x OBJECTIVE GRADING FREEZE — per-reading frozen direction/threshold (closes the S-obj-charts "freeze the grading inputs" deferral) (0055)

- **S-obj-freeze** (freeze the FULL per-reading KPI grading basis) — **closes the named S-obj-charts v1.x deferral: the per-reading RAG verdict was only PARTIALLY frozen, so a commitment revision that flipped `direction` (HIGHER↔LOWER) or moved the `at_risk_threshold` retroactively re-graded historical readings (a past green could read red).** Only `kpi_measurement.target_at_capture` was frozen; `direction`/`at_risk_threshold` were read LIVE from the governing commitment at serialize time. **What shipped (BACKEND-ONLY, additive, migration `0055`):** two new `kpi_measurement` columns — `direction_at_capture` (REUSE the `objective_direction` enum, `create_type=False`) + `at_risk_threshold_at_capture` (Numeric) — both **NULLABLE**; `record_measurement` snapshots them from the SAME governing-else-working resolution as `target_at_capture` (and seals them into the WORM `KPI_READING` record's `form_field_values` too); `api/objectives.py::_measurement` grades off the frozen columns (the two measurement endpoints SHED their now-dead `resolve_commitment` blocks — a net simplification). **The frozen basis stays INTERNAL** — the `Measurement` response is byte-identical (only the `rag` description in `openapi.yaml` updated to the frozen-basis semantics), so **no web/MSW change** (contracts + web CI jobs are no-ops). `compute_scorecard` (the live-objective grade) is intentionally UNCHANGED — a different semantic from a frozen historical reading. **Capture-time backfill (the load-bearing part — 4 Codex rounds hardened it):** each existing reading freezes the commitment that was the CURRENT effective version at its `created_at`, EXACTLY what `record_measurement` froze (it reads `current_effective_version_id`, which only moves at `_cutover`). The version-selection (1) considers ONLY versions that actually went Effective (`version_state='Effective' OR effective_to IS NOT NULL` — never a still-`Approved` scheduled revision), and (2) bounds each version's governing window by the PRIOR version's `effective_to` (the real cutover via the `superseded_by_version_id` chain), NOT its own (possibly scheduled) `effective_from`. A reading with no applicable governing version (pre-first-release) or under a fold-less/non-object snapshot falls back to the working row for BOTH columns (mirroring the runtime `governing = raw if isinstance(raw, dict) else None` guard). The backfill UPDATE runs as the migration OWNER role, unaffected by `kpi_measurement`'s app-role `REVOKE UPDATE,DELETE` (WORM intact — no app path updates the columns post-insert). **⚠ Traps a future session MUST carry:** (1) **the historical backfill is the hard part, not the column add** — reconstructing "what commitment governed at capture" is genuinely subtle and the migration round-trip / `alembic check` are VALUE-BLIND (empty fresh DB), so the **diff-critic + migration-reviewer were CLEAN on the application code but Codex was the edge-finder on the backfill over 4 rounds**: capture-time-vs-current → scheduled-vs-actual-cutover → not-yet-cutover-Approved-version → nullable-row-aborts-SET-NOT-NULL. Validate the backfill CASE/version-selection with **synthetic VALUES-based SQL** (no FK scaffolding) on a throwaway PG16 — pin the gap cases (a reading between a scheduled `effective_from` and the late `release_due` cutover must freeze the PRIOR basis). (2) **the migration-reviewer (not Codex) caught the FIRST real bug a clean diff-critic missed** — the threshold CASE was gated on a different predicate than the direction CASE (a `COALESCE` vs a `CASE`-with-fold-guard), so a fold-less governing version would NULL the threshold where the live grader uses the working row → gate BOTH columns on the IDENTICAL `jsonb_typeof(... ->'objective_commitment')='object'` predicate. (3) **`direction_at_capture` is NULLABLE, not NOT NULL** — `objective_id` is schema-nullable + the serializer handles an objective-less reading, so a `SET NOT NULL` would abort on an ad-hoc/imported objective-less row (Codex); the serializer narrows the now-Optional direction (None→"unmeasured", unreachable for the objective-scoped endpoints). (4) **the discriminating test must FLIP the verdict** — the existing target-freeze test coincidentally stays amber under both old/new behaviour; the new direction-flip + threshold-move integration tests are designed so frozen≠live (else they don't prove the freeze). (5) the **revision-id `varchar(32)` limit** — `0055_objective_freeze_grading_inputs` (36 chars) overflowed `alembic_version.version_num` at runtime (CI/round-trip would have caught it, but locally first) → renamed to `0055_objective_grading_freeze` (29). (6) the **ruff auto-fix hook strips a just-added import** (the recurring trap — re-add `objective_direction_enum` AFTER the column references it). **Deferred (named, not faked):** exposing the frozen `direction_at_capture`/`at_risk_threshold_at_capture` in the `Measurement` response (audit-transparency tooltip "graded under higher-is-better, amber<95" — owner kept them INTERNAL this slice; a trivial additive add); the other S-obj-charts deferrals (register sparklines, time-proportional x-axis, per-process objective reads) are untouched. **Test delta: +2 api unit** (`test_objective_measurement_rag.py` unit: `test_rag_reads_frozen_direction_not_live` + `test_rag_reads_frozen_threshold_not_live` — same value/target, opposite frozen basis → opposite verdict; full unit **810 passed**) **+2 api integration** (CI-only on Windows: a direction-flip and a threshold-move revision where frozen≠live → the old reading's `rag` is unchanged) + the `test_objective_revision.py` mid-revision capture test EXTENDED to assert direction/threshold snapshot the GOVERNING (not in-edit) values via a direct DB read; **web unchanged** (backend-only). ruff + mypy-strict clean; migration round-trip up↔down↔`alembic check` clean on PG16 + the backfill logic proven across 8 synthetic cases. **diff-critic CLEAN; migration-reviewer** found 1 MAJOR (the threshold/direction asymmetry, fixed + re-confirmed CLEAN); **Codex 4 rounds** (capture-time backfill / seal-record / contract-sync → prior-`effective_to` window → `version_state` filter + nullable → final **clean: "no major issues, Delightful"**); all 7 inline threads resolved. **Merge gate:** green CI (11/11) + 0 unresolved threads + Codex clean + owner's explicit go. PR [#197](https://github.com/CoJoA13/EasySynQ/pull/197), squash `b83bae4`.

## v1.x LEADERSHIP ROUTING — document-backed Top-Management release authorization (POL/OBJ/MR)

- **S-leadership-dcr-test** (lock in the DCR-implement leadership preflight — closes the named S-leadership-1 deferral) — **closes S-leadership-1's named-not-faked deferral verbatim: "a dedicated DCR-implement-of-a-leadership-artifact integration test."** **Backend TEST-ONLY — no migration (head stays `0055`, next free `0056`), no production code / contract / permission change.** **The guard:** `services/dcr/service.py::implement_dcr` preflights the Top-Management release gate SYNCHRONOUSLY (`services/vault/leadership_authorization.assert_release_authorized`, BEFORE `session.commit()`) for a REVISE/CREATE go-live — because the real cutover runs LATER in the async `release_due` Beat sweep, which **swallows** the gate's 409 (`vault/lifecycle.release_due` catches `(ProblemException, IllegalTransition)` and skips). Without the preflight a leadership-gated DCR implement would commit the DCR as Implemented while the version stayed Approved (a stuck false-success — the S-leadership-1 round-2 Codex P2). That path had ZERO DCR integration coverage (no `grep` hit for leadership / `assert_release_authorized` / `leadership_authorization_required` under `tests/integration`). **What shipped:** ONE integration test `test_revise_implement_blocked_until_leadership_authorized` (appended to `tests/integration/test_dcr_implement.py`) on a NON-singleton **OBJ**-typed REVISE DCR, both arms in one narrative: (1) flag on → implement → **409 `leadership_authorization_required`** AND `GET /dcrs/{id}` still `Approved` (the flip rolled back — no false Implemented); (2) authorize the Approved version (`POST …/request-leadership-authorization` + a Top-Management `verify`) → the **SAME** implement → 200 Implemented → `release_due()` sweep → version Effective → `close` → Closed (proves the preflight blocks, rolls back, AND does not over-block). **Care points / decisions:** the gate keys on `document_type.code ∈ {POL,OBJ,MR}` + the flag + a version `verify` sig, so a PLAIN OBJ-typed vault document (`s5.drive_to_approved(type_id("OBJ"))` + the existing REVISE `_drive_dcr_to_approved`, **NO `quality_objective` row**) exercises it — REVISE never trips the CREATE-only `create_target_managed_subtype`/`create_target_not_new` guards, and the generic sweep cutover has no managed-subtype coupling; **OBJ is `is_singleton=False`** so pushing it Effective is shared-org-safe (POL would be singleton → 409-only, no happy path — the owner's chosen "both arms on OBJ" over "blocked-only on POL"). The org flag is flipped ON in a `try`/reset OFF in `finally`, every assertion is run-scoped to this test's own ids, and `release_due()` is checked with membership (`in`), never an absolute global count (the global-sweep pattern of the existing `test_revise_implement_then_sweep_then_close`). Reuses the leadership helpers (`_set_leadership_flag`/`_assign_top_mgmt`/`_request`/`_my_pending_task`) cross-imported from `test_leadership_authorization` (aliased `_request as _request_leadership`, `_my_pending_task as _my_leadership_task` to dodge the `test_capa._my_pending_task` clash); identity wiring is consistent (same `keycloak_subject` → same `AppUser` across `_auth`/`_ensure_user`/`_assign_top_mgmt`). **⚠ Traps a future session MUST carry:** (1) the **ruff auto-fix hook STRIPPED the just-added imports as unused** (they're unused until a body references them) → append the test body FIRST, THEN re-add the imports (the recurring "ruff strips a just-added import" trap). (2) **CI scopes mypy to `src` only** (`ci.yml` api job: `uv run mypy src`), NOT `tests/` — so integration tests are NOT type-checked (this is why the pre-existing `no-any-return`/`attr-defined` "errors" in `test_vault`/`s5_helpers`/`test_capa`/`test_leadership_authorization` sit on green `main`); `ruff check .` DOES lint the whole repo (incl. tests). So a TEST-ONLY slice's local gate = ruff lint + format-check; mypy is irrelevant; the integration shards in CI are authoritative (the `-m integration` suite can't run on the owner's Windows box). (3) the discriminator: the **SAME** implement call is asserted 409 then 200 around the authorize step (same `_latest_approved_version` resolved both sides), so the verify-sig is genuinely load-bearing — not a coincidental pass. **Test delta: +1 api integration** (`test_dcr_implement.py`, +103 lines); **web unchanged** (backend-only). ruff + format clean; **diff-critic CLEAN** (verified the 409 code is raised in exactly one place — `leadership_authorization.py:187`; no earlier authz/SoD-2/FSM gate pre-empts it; the positive arm depends on the sig; flag-reset/run-scoping/`release_due`-membership all sound); **Codex CLEAN** ("Didn't find any major issues"); all 4 integration shards green. **Merge gate:** green CI (11/11) + 0 review threads + Codex clean + owner's explicit go. PR [#201](https://github.com/CoJoA13/EasySynQ/pull/201), squash `8d9dd08`.

- **S-leadership-cx1** (close the S-leadership-fe deferral **CX-1** — the server-computed per-document "request leadership authorization" capability) — **a small, FE-enabling follow-up that fixes a real visibility gap; backend probe + 1 contract field + FE gate rewire. NO migration (head stays `0054`), NO new permission key (rides the existing `document.approve`; R38 not engaged, R45-consistent); the welded `document_approval` path + `capabilities.release` byte-identical.** **The bug:** the `LeadershipReleaseGate` Request button gated on `usePermissions().can("document.approve")` with **no scope arg** → probes `/me/permissions` at **SYSTEM scope**; but `document.approve` has **`finest_scope=ARTIFACT`** (docs/07 §3.1 — the catalog even shows `Ken | document.approve | DOC_CLASS=Level-2`), so a content/process/folder/doc-class-scoped approver who legitimately holds it at the doc's scope (and whom the server WOULD authorize on POST) saw the dimmed "an approver must start it" message. A **visibility gap, not a security hole** (the request endpoint still enforces the real scope). **What shipped:** a server-computed, ABAC-aware boolean **`can_request`** on the `GET /documents/{id}/leadership-authorization` status payload the gate **already fetches** for POL/OBJ/MR alike (the FE now reads `data.can_request` and **drops `usePermissions` entirely**). The probe `_can_request_leadership_authorization` mirrors the request endpoint's gate (`_approve = require("document.approve", async_scope_resolver=_document_scope)`) **exactly**: `authorize(gather_grants("document.approve"), "document.approve", _document_scope_by_id(doc.id), ctx).allow` — **no `sig_hook`, no `process_ids`, the SoD overlay is inert** (the scope carries no author/approver, so a plain `authorize` matches the endpoint's effective result), and it does **NOT** fold in `is_leadership`/`required`/`Approved` state (those stay on the same payload; the FE ANDs them — the obsolete-capability-vs-§7.3-runtime-gate split). It uses the **pure PDP** (`gather_grants`+`authorize`), never `enforce` → **no authz-audit row** per detail GET. `release_authorization_status` stays caller-agnostic (the `can_request` merge is computed in the endpoint, which has the caller + `Request`). **Owner scoping call (the one real fork):** carry the boolean on the **status payload** (Option A) vs the document/objective/MR **`capabilities` block** (Option B, the brief's literal). Chose A — the gate already reads that exact payload for all three surfaces, so it's **1 serializer, 0 parent changes, no drift** across three independently-computed `capabilities.release` booleans; B would touch 3 cap serializers + 3 openapi schemas + thread a prop from 3 parents. **OpenAPI:** `can_request` added to `LeadershipAuthorizationStatus` `properties` **and** `required` (it has `additionalProperties:false`). **⚠ Codex P2 (the edge-finder again — a CLEAN diff-critic + web-test-trap-reviewer BOTH missed it):** the probe built `RequestContext` with **`source_ip=None`**, but the PDP rejects an **`ip_allow`-narrowed** `document.approve` grant when `source_ip` is None (`pdp.py:134`), while the real POST gate goes through the PEP's `evaluate()`, which sets `source_ip=request.client.host` (`pep.py:94`) → under an `ip_allow` grant the status returns `can_request:false` and the FE HIDES a button the server would allow — **the inverse false-PASS (over-strict probe) CX-1 itself exists to prevent.** Fixed (commit `8fedd64`) by threading `source_ip` into the probe exactly as `pep.py::evaluate` builds it, so an `ip_allow` grant evaluates identically in probe and gate. **⚠ Note for a future session:** `ip_allow` is a **v1-deferred** predicate (its `guest_grant` carrier is v1.x; no grant carries it today — docs/06), and the **sibling capability probes** (`release`/`edit`/… in `_document_capabilities`, `_objective_capabilities`, `_mr_capabilities`) **share the same pre-existing `source_ip` gap** → a separate, codebase-wide follow-up (thread `source_ip` through every capability probe, or leave it while `ip_allow` stays deferred); CX-1's probe now mirrors its endpoint exactly. The diff-critic had pre-verified the **SoD overlay is inert** here (it chased `document.approve` ∈ `_SOD_KEYS` → refuted: `_document_scope_by_id` never populates `author_user_id`/`approver_user_ids`, so both paths return identical allow/deny). **Deferred-still-open (named-not-faked):** CX-4 (the status read fails OPEN by design) and CR-2 (mock `:id` echo) from S-leadership-fe remain; the sibling-probe `source_ip` gap above. **Test delta: web 1041 → 1041** (FE test **reworked, not net-new** — `LeadershipReleaseGate.test.tsx` drops the `grantApprove()`/`/me/permissions` mock + `MePermissions` import and drives all 8 cases off the status fixtures' `can_request`; the in-progress/NEEDS_ATTENTION fixtures keep `can_request:true` so those suppression tests prove the STATE gate, not an accidental cap-false; one case uses `{...leadershipRequiredStatus, can_request:false}`); **+1 api integration** (`test_status_can_request_is_per_caller`: the requester [holds `document.approve`] reads `True`, the releaser [holds `document.release`+`read` but NOT `approve`] reads `False` — both can GET the status; run-scoped own-salt, flag-independent so no shared-DB flag manipulation) + a `can_request is False` assertion folded into the OBJ release happy-path. All 5 MSW status fixtures pinned to the real serializer (`satisfies`-enforced). `/check-web` green (eslint + strict `tsc` + build + **1041/1041**); ruff + mypy-strict clean; redocly valid; CI 11/11. **diff-critic + web-test-trap-reviewer CLEAN; Codex** found the 1 P2 (fixed) + re-reviewed clean. **Merge gate:** Codex thread resolved + green CI + owner's explicit go. PR [#195](https://github.com/CoJoA13/EasySynQ/pull/195), squash `6a14102`.

- **S-leadership-fe** (the deferred FRONT-END for the document-backed Top-Management RELEASE authorization, POL/OBJ/MR) — **completes the leadership-routing feature end-to-end; mirrors the S-improvement-4 review SPA.** **FE-only — no migration (head stays `0054`), no new endpoint/contract** (the backend #190 already shipped the `LeadershipAuthorization{Status,Cycle,Task,Request}` schemas + the two paths + the `WorkflowInstance.subject_type` enum member + the `OrgConfig` flag). **What shipped:** (1) a **`/tasks` decide arm** for a new `LEADERSHIP_AUTHORIZATION` task subject — `DecisionCard` gains verify/reject + the `verify` signature (structurally identical to `IMPROVEMENT_INITIATIVE`); a new `LeadershipApprovalContext` best-effort-reads `GET /documents/{id}` (calm 403 → falls back to the task's `subject_identifier`/`subject_title`); a `ReviewApprovePage` branch with **`isLeadership` added to the load-bearing `isDocumentSubject` negation** so a leadership task NEVER routes the welded document-approval stepper+redline (proven by a no-`workflow-instance`-read test); `useDecideTask` invalidates the leadership-authorization + doc/obj/MR caches. (2) a shared, **self-suppressing `LeadershipReleaseGate`** (new `features/leadership/` dir + `hooks.ts`) reading `GET /documents/{id}/leadership-authorization` — renders **null** for a non-leadership doc or flag-off (safe to embed everywhere); when `required && !authorized` it suppresses the Release button (because **`capabilities.release` does NOT fold in the gate** — verified in `_document_capabilities`, which computes `release` from `document.release`+SoD-2 only) and shows the state (authorized→release-may-proceed / awaiting / NEEDS_ATTENTION+re-request / a `document.approve`-gated Request button); embedded in ALL THREE release surfaces — **POL** via `ApprovalsTab`, **OBJ** via `ObjectiveDetailPage`, **MR** via `ManagementReviewDetailPage`, each guarding `canRelease` with `!blocksRelease`. The OBJ/MR Lifecycle-card render condition also includes `lead.blocksRelease` so the gate panel can't **collapse-and-vanish** when release is the only blocked affordance. **Owner scoping calls:** affordance on all three surfaces (one shared component) · inline panel above Release · admin org-flag toggle OUT of scope (set via `PATCH /admin/config`) · no dedicated pending-list (the `/tasks` inbox + the per-doc panel cover it). **⚠ Traps a future session MUST carry:** (1) **the orient fan-out reader GUESSED `action_expected:bool` and `stage_key:"top_management"` — BOTH WRONG**: `Task.action_expected` is a nullable `Text` (`string|null`) and the seeded stage key is `"leadership_authorization"` (migration 0054, `_STAGE["key"]`). Pin MSW fixtures to the REAL serializer (`api/documents.py::_leadership_authorization`), not the orient summary — and the **diff-critic** (not the clean web-test-trap-reviewer) caught the stage-key fixture mismatch. (2) **Codex (the edge-finder) found a real P1 a CLEAN diff-critic + web-test-trap-reviewer BOTH missed:** the leadership-authorization status is **version-scoped but cached by document id**, so a re-approval (release→revise→re-approve) would reuse the prior version's stale `authorized:true` → the gate would show Release for an unauthorized NEW version until the cutover 409s. Fix: the welded **DOCUMENT approval decision** (POL/OBJ/MR all re-approve through it) invalidates `["leadership-authorization", id]`. (3) **Codex > CodeRabbit** on this PR — Codex filed 5 (1×P1, 3×P2, 1×P3) incl. the P1; CodeRabbit filed 2 actionable + 1 nitpick; their **only overlap** was CR-1≡CX-5 (invalidate the status on the request 409 via **`onSettled`**, not just `onSuccess`). **Review fixes applied pre-merge** (commit `ca2be38`): CX-3 (the P1) · CX-2 (NEEDS_ATTENTION keeps the Request affordance — the backend permits a fresh cycle from a terminal NEEDS_ATTENTION instance) · CR-1/CX-5 (`onSettled` refetch) · CR-nit (assert the obj+MR invalidation keys). **Deferred (named-not-faked):** **CX-1** — the Request button gates on **SYSTEM-scope** `document.approve` because `DocumentSummary` carries no `process_id`, so a content/process-scoped approver doesn't see it (the server still enforces the real scope); the clean fix is a **server-computed per-document capability** (like `capabilities.release`) — a backend+FE follow-up (the v1 SYSTEM-fallback posture matches objectives/CAPA register gating; chip `task_500cf729`). **CX-4** — the status read **fails OPEN** by design (a transient leadership-status read failure → `blocksRelease=false` → Release shows; failing closed would couple EVERY document's Release to that one endpoint's availability, and the backend cutover is the real gate; the protected persona — can release OBJ/MR but lacks `document.read` — is effectively empty). **CR-2** — echo `:id` in the request mock (low value; no test reads the response body). **Test delta: web 1017 → 1041 (+24)** — `LeadershipReleaseGate.test.tsx` (8: self-suppress / required+Request / no-`document.approve`-hides-Request / awaiting / NEEDS_ATTENTION+re-request / authorized / non-Approved-null / 409-calm+refetch), a `ReviewApprovePage` LEADERSHIP_AUTHORIZATION describe (context+verify-card / no-welded-path / sign-gating / decided-alert / axe), a `DecisionCard` variant, the `useDecideTask` LEADERSHIP_AUTHORIZATION + DOCUMENT-CX-3 invalidation tests, and the gate integration on ApprovalsTab/OBJ/MR. `/check-web` green (eslint + strict `tsc` + build + full vitest **1041/1041**). **web-test-trap-reviewer CLEAN; diff-critic** caught the stage-key fixture + flagged the OBJ/MR card-collapse coverage (both fixed pre-PR). **Merge gate:** all 7 Codex+CodeRabbit threads resolved (fixed or deferred-with-rationale) + 0 unresolved + green CI + owner's explicit go. PR [#192](https://github.com/CoJoA13/EasySynQ/pull/192), squash `9681b6d`.

- **S-leadership-1** (route the clause-5/6/9 leadership artifacts' release sign-off to a Top-Management pool) — **the document-backed sibling of S-improvement-4, closing the named-enhancement gap the welded single-stage `document_approval` can't express (doc 10 §2.5, decisions-register R45).** **Why:** the Quality Policy (POL §5.2), Quality Objectives (OBJ §6.2) and Management Review (MR §9.3) are all `kind=DOCUMENT` and rode the welded single-stage path — their approver pool is whoever holds `document.approve` (Approver/QMS-Owner); there was NO Top-Management gate on these leadership acts, and the welded `decide()` cannot conditionally route. **What shipped (BACKEND-ONLY, opt-in, additive — the welded `document_approval`/`decide()`/`instantiate_approval` path is byte-identical, pinned by `test_approval.py`):** an org config flag `system_config.leadership_release_requires_top_management_authorization` (default **OFF**) that, when on, blocks **RELEASE** of a leadership-type doc until a "Top Management" member signs `meaning=verify` on the Approved `document_version`. The gate is enforced at the **shared `vault/lifecycle._cutover` chokepoint** (covers the direct `POST /documents/{id}/release`, the `/objectives/{id}/release` + `/management-reviews/{id}/release` wrappers, AND the DCR-implement go-live — one insertion, no side-door) via a shared `services/vault/leadership_authorization.assert_release_authorized`. The sign-off routes through the generic multi-stage engine: `POST /documents/{id}/request-leadership-authorization` (gate `document.approve` at the doc scope; 409 `not_a_leadership_artifact` / `document_not_approved` / `authorization_in_progress` / `already_authorized`) opens a seeded `leadership_release_authorization` instance (a single Top-Management `PARALLEL`/`ANY` stage, `signature={meaning:verify}`) under a NEW `WorkflowSubjectType.LEADERSHIP_AUTHORIZATION`; the decision rides the existing `POST /tasks/{id}/decision` (a new dispatch branch → `decide_leadership_authorization`, which allow-lists `{verify,reject}` AFTER the keyless candidate-pool auth gate and forces `REJECTED`+skips siblings on a decisive decline). The COMPLETING `verify` mints a SINGLE `signature_event(meaning=verify, signed_object_type=document_version, signed_object_id=<version>)` bound to the EXISTING version row (NO own-table stage-event → **no two-INSERT seam**, unlike CAPA/initiative) + a first-class `EventType.LEADERSHIP_AUTHORIZED` audit (object_type=`version`); the document FSM is **untouched** (release is the separate act the cutover gates). Plus a `GET /documents/{id}/leadership-authorization` status read (`{is_leadership_artifact, required, version_id, authorized, instance}`). **Authority = Top-Management candidate-pool membership (NO new permission key — R38; the self-scoped-task doctrine); the REQUEST reuses `document.approve`.** **Migration 0054** (head `0053→0054`): the two enum `ADD VALUE`s (`workflow_subject_type`→`LEADERSHIP_AUTHORIZATION`, `event_type`→`LEADERSHIP_AUTHORIZED`) in an `autocommit_block` so the new subject-type commits before the seed uses it (the 0053 ordering rule); `signed_object_type=document_version` + the `verify` meaning are **REUSED** (no enum adds there — R2 closed-but-additive); an `ALTER TABLE system_config ADD COLUMN … boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false` (mirrored in the ORM); and a seed-only `leadership_release_authorization` workflow_definition + its `leadership_authorization` stage **reusing the 0038 "Top Management" role** (NOT reseeded). Round-trips up↔down↔`alembic check` clean; NOT-EXISTS-guarded downgrade. **OpenAPI:** the two new document endpoints + 4 schemas (`LeadershipAuthorizationRequest`/`Task`/`Cycle`/`Status`), `LEADERSHIP_AUTHORIZATION` added to `WorkflowInstance.subject_type`, the config flag on `OrgConfig`/`OrgConfigUpdate`. **The Codex saga (5 findings / 2 rounds — a CLEAN diff-critic + migration-reviewer + all local gates missed every one; the edge-finder again):** **round 1 (3×P2 + 1×P3):** (a) the single-active guard only blocks NON-terminal instances → a 2nd request after a COMPLETED authorization (pre-release) mints a DUPLICATE `verify` sig → the `scalar_one_or_none()` replay re-derivation 500s → fixed by guarding the request with `has_release_authorization` (409 `already_authorized`; **version-scoped**, so a NEW Approved version still re-requests); (b) the new `WorkflowSubjectType` must be added to the openapi `WorkflowInstance.subject_type` enum (`GET /workflow-instances/{id}` + `GET /tasks/{id}` serialize it → a generated client would reject the response); (c) the decision result used `version_id` but `DecisionResult` is `additionalProperties:false` and defines `document_version_id` → renamed the result key (COMPLETE + replay) + the tests; (d/P3) a decisive reject left `stage_state=PENDING` while forcing `current_state=REJECTED` → set `stage_state=FAILED`. **round 2 (1×P2):** the **DCR-implement go-live** of a leadership artifact runs the cutover only later in the async `release_due` sweep, which CATCHES the gate's `ProblemException` and skips → `implement_dcr` would commit the DCR as Implemented while the version stays Approved (false success, stuck) → extracted the gate to the shared `assert_release_authorized` (the cutover delegates) and **preflight it synchronously in `implement_dcr`** before scheduling `effective_from`/committing. ⚠ **Traps a future session MUST carry:** (1) **the ANY-quorum + outcome-allow-list pair from S-improvement-4 carry verbatim** — allow-list `{verify,reject}` AFTER the keyless candidate-pool gate (else a generic positive mints a spurious verify; a pre-auth 422 leaks the task), and an ANY decline is NOT decisive in the engine → force `REJECTED`+skip siblings in the caller. (2) **the release-gate read of the hot shared `system_config` row lives in the SERIALIZABLE `_cutover`** — acceptable (the integration suite is sequential per pytest-split shard; a genuinely concurrent `/admin/config` write would 40001 a release, which is retryable), but a v1.x option is to read the flag pre-cutover. (3) **POL is `is_singleton=True` (R25)** — the shared integration org tolerates exactly ONE Effective POL, so the **full release→Effective happy-path was put on NON-singleton OBJ**; POL covers only the gate-blocked-409 + non-releasing assertions (it is never pushed Effective). (4) **a new `WorkflowSubjectType` whose `subject_id` is a `documented_information` id needs NO `subject_label`/My-Tasks change** — the existing DocumentedInformation fallthrough/outerjoin already resolves it. **Deferred (named-not-faked):** the **front-end** (request/decide UI + the release-gate affordance) — the next slice; **`quorum: ALL`** (doc 10 §2.5's worked example — shipped as `ANY`, consistent with the CAPA `crit_topmgmt` + initiative precedent); the **import-baseline path** is intentionally ungated (a baseline import is not a release; it never hits `_cutover`); a dedicated **DCR-implement-of-a-leadership-artifact** integration test (the preflight reuses the gate proven by the direct/OBJ/MR tests; a clean DCR-revise-of-a-leadership-doc fixture is high-cost — POL is singleton, OBJ/MR have bespoke revision flows). **Test delta: +12 api integration** (`test_leadership_authorization.py`: OBJ full request→verify→release-Effective with the verify signature + LEADERSHIP_AUTHORIZED audit; flag-off ungated; POL recognized + release-blocked-409; ordinary-doc [SOP] untouched with the flag on; request guards [not-leadership / not-approved / twice / already-authorized]; decline-decisive + re-requestable + allow-list-422; non-member 404-collapse; idempotent verify replay; MR release-gated). **web unchanged** (backend-only). Full unit `808 passed`; migration round-trip + `alembic check` clean; redocly valid; ruff + mypy-strict clean (only the pre-existing Windows `O_NOFOLLOW` source nit). **diff-critic + migration-reviewer CLEAN** pre-PR. **Merge gate:** all Codex threads addressed across 2 rounds + green CI (11/11) + owner's explicit go. PR [#190](https://github.com/CoJoA13/EasySynQ/pull/190), squash `9ee99b2`.

## v1.x RENDER HARDENING — pre-render linked-resource detection + gotenberg 8.34 (no migration)
